Decision: Added to `requirements.txt`, pinned to versions actually installed and verified working this round: `fastapi==0.141.1`, `starlette==1.6.0` (FastAPI's own ASGI dependency, pinned explicitly rather than left to float — same reasoning as DECISIONS #24's explicit `httpx`/`httpx2` pinning), `uvicorn[standard]==0.52.4` (ASGI server to actually run the app), `python-multipart==0.0.32` (required by FastAPI for `UploadFile`/multipart form parsing — undeclared transitive dependency otherwise), `python-docx==1.2.0` (DOCX paragraph/heading-style parsing for the upload chunker, DECISIONS #99).
Why: `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s "pin base image versions" spirit is already applied to every other dependency in this file (`openai==3.3.1`, `pypdf==6.16.1`, etc) — no `:latest`-equivalent unpinned entries. `httpx==0.28.1` was already present (DECISIONS #24, added ahead of need for FastAPI's `TestClient`) and needed no change.
Date: 2026-08-22

## #103 — `rag-review` pass on `app/chunking/uploaded_document.py`: 2 HIGH fixes applied
Decision: Two HIGH findings from a pre-commit `rag-review` pass on the new upload chunker were fixed, both independently re-verified in the actual code (not taken on the reviewer's word alone) before this entry was written:
- **PDF page-boundary splitting.** `_paragraphs_from_pdf` previously extracted and split each PDF page's text independently, then flattened the per-page paragraph lists — so a clause spanning a page break (no blank line at the seam, routine in agenda packets and grant guidelines) got forced into two separate "paragraphs" before grouping ever saw them, defeating the never-split-mid-thought guarantee at exactly the boundary it exists to protect. Fixed: every page's `extract_text()` output is now joined into one document string with `"\n"` first, and the blank-line paragraph split runs once, globally, over the joined text — a page-spanning clause with no blank line at the seam now correctly merges into one paragraph; a genuine paragraph break that happens to land at a page end still splits correctly either way, since the blank-line pattern survives the join.
- **Oversized-paragraph escape hatch.** A single paragraph exceeding `app.embeddings.client`'s real embedding-input character ceiling (minus a safety margin — `MAX_SAFE_CHUNK_TEXT_CHARS`) had no handling before this fix; grouping alone could not have prevented an oversized single paragraph from reaching the embeddings API and hitting `EmbeddingInputTooLargeError` mid-upload. `_split_oversized_text_if_needed()` now catches this case: prefers a sentence-boundary split, falls back to a hard fixed-size character window only when no sentence boundary exists within the oversized text (true last resort, not the default path). A split piece's `section_label` is suffixed `" (split N/M)"` (deliberately distinct from the normal `" (part N)"` multi-paragraph-group suffix, so a citation visibly signals "unusually large source content had to be mechanically split," not confused with ordinary grouping), a `logger.warning` fires, and `chunk_id`s get a `:split{N}` suffix — still fully deterministic (DECISIONS #58/#99), so a retry reprocesses into the same IDs.

Two smaller follow-ups landed in the same pass: a docstring correction (`CHUNK_TARGET_SIZE_CHARS`'s comment had overstated DECISIONS #92 as evidence about uploaded-document chunk sizes specifically, when #92 actually measured the 8 crawler chunkers' output — the number itself was unaffected, only the justification text was wrong); and a `logger.debug` added when `_sections_from_docx` finds zero `Heading N`-styled paragraphs in a DOCX over 5 paragraphs long (a real, silent-until-now signal that a DOCX's structure didn't match what the heading-based chunking path expects).
Why: The PDF-splitting bug is exactly the "don't split mid-thought" failure `.claude/rules/rag.md` exists to catch, just surfacing at a page boundary instead of an HTML/PDF-structure boundary the way DECISIONS #59/#66 already caught it elsewhere. The oversized-paragraph gap was a real, unhandled path to a mid-upload failure with no signal beyond a raw exception — the same "fail loud, but also handle the case you can reasonably handle" discipline `.claude/rules/crawler.md` established for Whisper's file-size ceiling (DECISIONS #86) now applied to the embeddings API's own input ceiling.
Date: 2026-08-22

## #104 — Real end-to-end verification: upload a real file, confirm idempotent re-upload, confirm it's queryable with a correct citation
Decision: Live, non-mocked verification (orchestrator, this entry) of PLAN.md Phase H's own acceptance test — "an uploaded doc is queryable with citations immediately after" — using FastAPI's `TestClient` against `app.api.main.app` with real dependencies (no `get_db_conn`/`get_openai_client` overrides): a small real `.txt` file containing one fact not present anywhere else in the corpus (a unique verification string) was POSTed to `/documents/upload`. Confirmed: initial response `202`/`status="processing"`; the background task completed synchronously within the same `TestClient` call in this environment, landing `document_uploads.status="completed"`, `chunk_count=1`; a second, identical upload attempt returned `200` (not `202`) with the cached `"completed"` record and the server log line confirming "already completed — short-circuit, no reprocessing" — no second embedding API call was made (DECISIONS #98's idempotency contract, confirmed live, not just by inspection). Immediately after, `POST /query` asking specifically for that fact returned `not_in_corpus=False`, the correct answer containing the exact verification string, and one citation whose `source_url` was `upload://<the file's real sha256>` and whose `doc_type` was `"uploaded_document"` — confirming DECISIONS #97/#99's `chunk_id`/attribution design resolves correctly end to end, not just in isolated unit tests. The test upload's `document_uploads` and `chunks` rows were deleted after verification — this was a real API call against the live corpus DB, not left behind as permanent test data mixed into real content.
Why: Every prior phase this session (crawlers, chunking, embeddings, retrieval/generation, Granicus) was validated against real data before being called done, per this project's own established discipline — Phase H's own build round produced 12 new DECISIONS entries (#97-103) of real design reasoning but no live end-to-end run, which is the one thing PLAN.md's own Phase H done-criteria explicitly names as the acceptance bar. Closing that gap before considering Phase H complete, rather than treating "the code compiles and the mocked tests pass" as equivalent to "the feature works" — the same standard this project has held itself to throughout.
Date: 2026-08-22

## #105 — Web UI served as static files by FastAPI itself, no separate UI container
Decision: The Phase I ask-box+citations page is plain HTML + vanilla JS, served by the existing FastAPI app (`app.api.main`) via a `StaticFiles` mount, calling the already-live `/query`, `/health`, and `/documents/upload` endpoints client-side. No separate `ui` service/container, no Node/React/bundler build step.
Why: PLAN.md's own Phase I done-criteria says "no design system, just usable," and a static page has zero reason to run as its own process — FastAPI already serves HTTP and already has CORS/origin handled trivially by being same-origin. A 4th container (or even a 3rd, a dedicated `ui` service) would mean a second image-or-build-step, a second place secrets/env could leak, and a second thing that can crash-loop, for a page whose entire job is "fetch JSON, render it" — no justification for that cost at this project's scale. This keeps DECISIONS #8's "one image" story completely literal: one Dockerfile, one image, role selected by `docker-compose.yml`'s `command:` per service (`api`/`worker`), and the UI isn't even a third role — it's static assets the `api` role already serves.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #106 — `app/db/connection.py` DB host becomes env-var driven (`LOCAL_DB_HOST`), default unchanged
Decision: `get_connection()`'s `psycopg.connect()` call now reads `host=os.environ.get("LOCAL_DB_HOST", "127.0.0.1")` instead of the hardcoded literal `"127.0.0.1"`. Every existing host-run caller (pytest, `uvicorn app.api.main:app --reload`, dev scripts) is unaffected with zero `.env` changes required, since the default is identical to the old hardcoded value. `docker-compose.yml`'s new `api`/`worker` services set `LOCAL_DB_HOST=db` in their `environment:` block, pointing at the Compose network's `db` service hostname instead.
Why: Inside a container, `127.0.0.1` resolves to the container itself, not the `db` service — a hardcoded value that worked for every host-run process this project has had so far would silently fail to connect once anything ran containerized. `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s "one image, config-driven environments" rule names exactly this shape of fix: an env var with a safe default, not a build-time branch or a second code path.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #107 — One Dockerfile, pinned `python:3.12.10-slim`, role selected entirely by docker-compose `command:`
Decision: A single repo-root `Dockerfile` builds one image — pinned to `python:3.12.10-slim` (exact match to `.venv`'s confirmed `python --version` output, avoiding host/container interpreter drift), installs `requirements.txt`, copies the full `app/` tree. No `CMD`/`ENTRYPOINT` bakes in a role; `docker-compose.yml`'s `api` service runs `uvicorn app.api.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000` and its `worker` service runs `python -m app.granicus.run_worker` (DECISIONS #109) off the exact same built image. A `.dockerignore` (excluding `.venv/`, `.git/`, `.env`, `tests/`, `scripts/`, and the top-level `DECISIONS.md`/`PLAN.md`/`CLAUDE.md` doc files by name) keeps the build context lean and `.env` out of the image by construction, on top of it never being `COPY`'d.
Why: DECISIONS #8 already committed this project to "one image, config-driven environments, no separate builds per role" — this is that decision's literal Dockerfile implementation. `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s "no `:latest`" rule is honored by pinning the exact patch version already proven working in dev, not just a `3.12` floating tag.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #108 — `docker-compose.yml`: `api`/`worker` services added, gated on `db`'s healthcheck, secrets via root `.env` interpolation
Decision: Two new services added alongside the existing `db`: `api` (built from the new Dockerfile, `command: uvicorn app.api.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000`, port bound `127.0.0.1:8000:8000` — localhost only, matching `db`'s own binding, not `0.0.0.0` — exposed to the host) and `worker` (same build, `command: python -m app.granicus.run_worker`, no port mapping — not an HTTP service). Both set `depends_on: db: condition: service_healthy` (gating startup on `db`'s existing `pg_isready` healthcheck actually passing, not just the container starting) and both get `LOCAL_DB_HOST=db` plus `${VAR}`-interpolated passthrough of `LOCAL_DB_USER`/`PASSWORD`/`NAME`/`PORT`/`OPENAI_API_KEY` from the root `.env` — no secret value is hardcoded into the compose file itself. `db`'s existing `127.0.0.1:${LOCAL_DB_PORT}:5432` host-only port bind needed no change: live-verified that Compose's internal service-to-service DNS/network (which `api`/`worker` use to reach `db` by service name) is independent of the host port binding.
Why: `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s "secrets never live in committed config" rule is satisfied by `.env`-sourced interpolation rather than literal values in the compose file. Gating on `service_healthy` rather than plain `depends_on` (which only waits for "container started," not "Postgres is actually accepting connections") avoids a first-boot race where `api`/`worker` try to connect before Postgres is ready. Live-verified 2026-08-23: `docker compose up --build -d db api` brought `db` healthy then `api` up, and `curl http://localhost:8000/health` returned a real `{"status":"ok","db":"ok"}` — confirming actual container-to-container DB reachability, not just that the compose file parses.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #109 — Granicus worker container entrypoint: `app/granicus/run_worker.py`, 30-second poll interval
Decision: New file `app/granicus/run_worker.py`, runnable as `python -m app.granicus.run_worker`, wraps the existing bounded-batch `process_pending_jobs()` (DECISIONS #12/#79-81/#86 — explicitly not a service by its own docstring) in a `while not shutdown` loop: `get_connection()` → `process_pending_jobs(max_jobs=1)` → interruptible `threading.Event.wait(30.0)` → repeat, with `get_connection()` itself inside the per-cycle try/except (DECISIONS #111 below — a fix applied during pre-commit review, not the original shape). SIGTERM/SIGINT set the shutdown event, checked before claiming the next job and unblocking the sleep immediately, so `docker-compose down` stops it promptly rather than waiting out a sleep. `max_jobs=1` per cycle (not the whole pending queue) so the shutdown flag is re-checked after every single job completes rather than only after a whole batch (a job can run up to ~19 minutes per the worker's own stale-claim-threshold analysis). `AuthenticationError`/`PermissionDeniedError` and other unexpected exceptions (including a failed `get_connection()`) are logged at ERROR with full detail and the loop continues rather than exiting — a bad API key or a transient DB blip won't fix itself by crash-looping, and an in-flight job is already reverted to `pending` by `worker.py`'s existing stale-recovery path, so nothing is silently lost. Poll-cycle logging: DEBUG on a genuinely empty cycle, INFO heartbeat once per 60 consecutive empty cycles (~30 min), WARNING heartbeat once per 60 consecutive *errored* cycles (tracked separately from empty cycles so a string of DB failures doesn't get logged as if the queue were merely quiet) — so a long-running idle container doesn't flood logs while still proving it's alive, and a long-running *broken* container doesn't get misread as merely idle.
Why: **30 seconds, derived, not a round-number default.** Cost side: the live `granicus_transcription_jobs` table currently has zero real rows and, at the project's own 12-month-backfill bound (DECISIONS #12), tops out around ~52 rows/year — one `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 1` every 30s (2,880/day) against a table that small is not measurable DB load on any tier this project runs, whether the queue is empty (the common case today) or not. Latency side: a real job's own processing time (worker.py's own documented ~19-minute typical worst case) dwarfs a 30-second pickup delay once Amber actually registers a meeting, so 30s clears "starts within a normal work session" with room to spare. Sub-10s was rejected as buying no perceptible latency improvement for ~6x the idle-poll volume; minutes-to-hourly was rejected as reading like the pipeline is broken to someone who just ran the registration CLI and is watching for it to pick up. `max_jobs=1` (rather than draining the whole queue per cycle) exists specifically so a container can be stopped/restarted within roughly one job's duration, not one whole batch's duration, under `docker-compose down`.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #110 — Web UI implementation: static-file layout, `not_in_corpus` display, upload form included, output escaped via `textContent`
Decision: `app/api/static/{index.html,style.css,app.js}` implement PLAN.md's Phase I page. `app/api/main.py` mounts `StaticFiles(directory="app/api/static", html=True)` at `"/"`, added AFTER every `app.include_router(...)` call — Starlette matches explicit routes before falling through to a trailing `Mount`, live-verified (`GET /openapi.json` still lists exactly the 4 API routes; `/`, `/style.css`, `/app.js` serve 200; an unmatched path returns StaticFiles' own 404, not a silent 200). `not_in_corpus: true` responses render in a visually distinct, non-alarming block ("This isn't covered in the indexed corpus yet") separate from the genuine-error state (4xx/5xx, styled as an error) — both are handled distinctly from the normal-answer state, and the JS's `extractErrorMessage()` handles both this app's own `{"detail": "<string>"}` `HTTPException` bodies and Pydantic's `{"detail": [{"msg": ...}, ...]}` validation-error array shape (verified live against a real blank-question 422 and a real missing-field 422 — a naive single-shape reader would have rendered `[object Object]` on the array case). The upload form was built (PLAN.md's "if time allows" was met, not treated as a blocker on the core ask-box+citations path, which was built and verified first). Every API-response field rendered into the page (`answer`, citation `section_label`/`source_url`/`doc_type`, upload filenames) uses `textContent`/`element.href` assignment, never `innerHTML` — corpus content can originate from `POST /documents/upload`'s arbitrary user-supplied files, so answer/citation text is treated as untrusted for rendering even though it's served through this app's own backend.
Why: DECISIONS #105 already settled "static files via FastAPI, no separate container" — this entry covers the concrete implementation choices within that. The `not_in_corpus` visual distinction is the UI-layer expression of `.claude/rules/rag.md`'s grounding-contract rule ("the response says so explicitly ... never falls through to general knowledge") — a user seeing red/error styling on an honest "not covered" answer would misread a correctly-working grounding contract as a bug. The `textContent`-only rendering choice is a concrete, cheap XSS-prevention measure given this project's own upload path is a real (if currently unauthenticated — see CLAUDE.md's open item) channel for user-supplied content to reach other users' rendered pages.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #111 — `crawler-review` pass on `app/granicus/run_worker.py`: 1 HIGH fixed, 1 LOW fixed
Decision: `crawler-review` ran an adversarial pass on the new worker-entrypoint file before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate. Both findings independently re-verified against the actual code before this entry was written, not taken on the reviewer's word alone.

**HIGH, applied — the DB connection call sat outside its own try/except.** `get_connection()` was called before the per-cycle `try:` block in `run_forever()`; if it raised (the exact "DB restart, network blip" scenario the module's own docstring names as the reason for opening a fresh connection every cycle), the exception propagated straight out of `run_forever()` and killed the process — no ERROR log, and the `worker` compose service has no `restart:` policy, so this was a silent, unrecoverable death from a single transient DB hiccup. Directly contradicted the module's own documented "no exception type in this loop is treated as fatal" contract. Fixed: `conn = get_connection()` moved inside the try, `conn` initialized to `None` beforehand so the `finally: if conn is not None: conn.close()` guard doesn't itself raise on a failed connect. A connect failure now falls through to the existing generic `except Exception` handler — logged loud, loop continues at the normal interval.

**LOW, applied — heartbeat log conflated "queue truly empty" with "erroring every cycle."** A cycle that raised (any exception, including the connect-failure case above) set `results = []`, identical to a genuine empty poll — so 60 consecutive errored cycles would log "queue empty for 60 consecutive cycles," misleadingly implying nothing was wrong. Fixed: `run_forever()` now tracks `cycle_errored` and two separate counters (`empty_cycles_since_heartbeat` / `errored_cycles_since_heartbeat`); an errored streak gets its own WARNING-level heartbeat naming the actual condition, distinct from the INFO-level "genuinely idle" heartbeat.

Why: `.claude/rules/crawler.md`'s "fail loud, never silently empty" rule extends naturally to this loop's own health signal — the HIGH finding is the sharpest version of that failure (silent process death, not just a silent log), and the LOW finding is the same principle applied to the heartbeat's own honesty. Both are real, load-bearing fixes: "no exception type in this loop is treated as fatal" (the module's own documented contract) didn't hold if the single most likely real-world failure — a DB blip — bypassed the try entirely.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #112 — `deploy-review` pass on the deploy layer diff (`.dockerignore`, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `app/db/connection.py`): 1 HIGH fixed, 1 MEDIUM fixed, 1 LOW fixed
Decision: `deploy-review` ran an adversarial pass on the deploy layer before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate. All findings independently re-verified against the actual code before this entry was written, not taken on the reviewer's word alone.

**HIGH, applied — test-only dependencies were shipping in the runtime image.** `requirements.txt`'s single `pip install -r requirements.txt` in the Dockerfile pulled `pytest`/`responses` into both the `api` and `worker` containers — the single-stage build had no separation between runtime and dev dependencies. Fixed: `pytest==9.1.1`/`responses==0.26.2` moved to a new `requirements-dev.txt`; the Dockerfile is unchanged (still installs only `requirements.txt`), so the runtime image no longer carries test tooling. Confirmed nothing else in the repo referenced `requirements.txt`'s old pytest/responses lines by grep before the move.

**MEDIUM, applied — `api` service's port bind was wide-open where `db`'s was not.** `docker-compose.yml`'s `db` service binds `127.0.0.1:${LOCAL_DB_PORT}:5432` (localhost only); the new `api` service's `ports: "8000:8000"` bound all interfaces, making it LAN-reachable — inconsistent posture, and notable given CLAUDE.md's own standing caveat that `/documents/upload` has no auth yet. Fixed: changed to `127.0.0.1:8000:8000`, matching `db`'s existing localhost-only convention. This is a local-dev compose file only, not the Fargate deploy path.

**LOW, applied — DECISIONS #107's own prose didn't match `.dockerignore`'s actual contents.** #107 claimed the file excludes `docs/`; no `docs/` directory exists in this repo, and the real exclusions are `DECISIONS.md`/`PLAN.md`/`CLAUDE.md` by name. Harmless (the described directory was never real) but a decision-log/code mismatch is exactly the kind of drift this log exists to prevent from compounding — #107's text corrected in place rather than left standing.

The web UI layer (`app/api/main.py`'s `StaticFiles` mount, `app/api/static/*`) had no dedicated specialist reviewer available — no `api-review` agent exists in this project's roster — and was self-reviewed by the orchestrator directly against `.claude/rules/rag.md`'s XSS/untrusted-content concerns (already named in DECISIONS #110) and general correctness. No findings survived that pass.

Why: "one image, config-driven" (DECISIONS #8, `.claude/rules/deploy.md`) doesn't hold if dev tooling rides along into the runtime image — the HIGH finding is a direct violation of that standing rule, not a style nitpick. The MEDIUM port-binding finding is a straightforward consistency/least-exposure fix given this project's own already-documented no-auth caveat on the upload endpoint. Catching both before commit rather than after is the entire point of this repo's pre-commit review gate.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #113 — Added `api-review` specialist; closes the reviewer-roster gap DECISIONS #112 flagged
Decision: New agent `.claude/agents/api-review.md`, modeled on `rag-review`/`deploy-review`/`crawler-review`'s existing shape (charter, severity-ranked findings, explicit "what you do NOT do" lane boundaries). Scope: FastAPI route/response-model correctness, exception-to-HTTP-status mapping, untrusted-input handling at the API boundary (including any static/UI assets the same app serves), route/mount ordering, and idempotency at the API layer. `cba-rag.md`'s orchestrator description and its "Review cadence" section, plus `CLAUDE.md`'s specialist roster line, all updated to list it alongside the other three reviewers.
Why: DECISIONS #110's web UI diff and DECISIONS #97-104's Phase H FastAPI diff both landed without a domain-matched adversarial review pass — the orchestrator self-reviewed in both cases for lack of an available specialist, explicitly noted as a gap in DECISIONS #112 ("no `api-review` agent exists in this project's roster"). Every other code-touching domain in this project (retrieval/prompt, deploy/infra, crawler/transcription) already has a dedicated reviewer per `.claude/agents/`; the API layer was the one load-bearing surface without one, despite being the most externally-reachable one (the only currently-unauthenticated HTTP surface, per CLAUDE.md's own standing caveat). Closing this now rather than continuing to route API-layer reviews through generic self-review.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #114 — `api-review`'s first real pass, run retroactively against the already-committed Phase H/I diff: 2 HIGH fixed, 2 MEDIUM fixed
Decision: Immediately after `api-review` (DECISIONS #113) was created, it was run against the full `app/api/` diff across both commits that built it (Phase H's routes, Phase I's UI) — a deliberate smoke test of whether the new agent surfaces real findings, not a formality. It did. All four findings were independently re-verified against the actual code before this entry was written, not taken on the reviewer's word alone. No CRITICAL findings; no unescaped output, no leaked secrets, no injection path.

**HIGH, applied — the upload/embed idempotency contract had a real TOCTOU race.** `app/api/documents.py`'s upload pre-check (`SELECT` then `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT`) and `app/embeddings/pipeline.py`'s `chunk_already_embedded` pre-check were both plain check-then-act with no lock. Two callers racing on the same file_hash (or the same chunk_id, reachable from two racing background tasks) could both read "not yet done" and both pay for an OpenAI embedding call before either committed — DB state still converged correctly (`ON CONFLICT`), but the API call itself isn't refundable. This is a direct violation of `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s idempotency-key-and-pre-check rule as written, which the module docstrings had claimed was fully satisfied. Fixed in both places with `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(%s)::bigint)` acquired before the read: transaction-scoped (auto-releases on commit/rollback, needs no explicit unlock), and fits both modules' existing commit-per-unit-of-work boundaries exactly — a second caller on the same key blocks until the first's transaction ends, then its own pre-check correctly sees what the first one just committed. Verified directly, not just by code inspection: a new test (`tests/db/test_pipeline.py::test_advisory_lock_serializes_concurrent_sessions_on_the_same_key`) opens two real, independent connections, has one hold the lock across an uncommitted transaction, proves a second session genuinely blocks on the same key (not just that the SQL parses), then proves it unblocks the instant the first commits.

**HIGH, applied — two routes had zero exception handling, unlike every other route in this layer.** `GET /sources/{doc_id}` and part of `POST /documents/upload`'s pre-check/upsert had no try/except at all — a DB error fell through to Starlette's default handler, which returns plain text ("Internal Server Error"), not this API's otherwise-uniform `{"detail": ...}` JSON shape, and gets logged through uvicorn's generic error logger instead of this app's own per-module `logger`. Fixed: both now catch broadly, log with request-identifying context (`doc_id`/`file_hash`), and raise the same generic-500 `HTTPException` shape `query.py`'s catch-all already uses — consistent with `.claude/rules/rag.md`'s "treat failures deliberately, don't let one bad path fall through to something undifferentiated" spirit, applied at the API boundary generally, not just to LLM-call failures.

**MEDIUM, applied — the 20MB upload limit was enforced only after the full body was already buffered into memory.** Self-documented as a known gap in the original code (correctly assigning the real fix — a proxy/ALB-level body-size cap — to deploy-infra, not fixed there yet). `upload_document()` now checks the request's `Content-Length` header and rejects before ever calling `file.read()` when the client sends one — closes the gap for any honest client (browsers/curl send a real Content-Length for a multipart upload). Explicitly does NOT close it for a client that omits Content-Length or uses chunked transfer-encoding — the proxy-level limit remains deploy-infra's open item, and the code comment now says so instead of implying the app-level check alone was sufficient.

**MEDIUM, applied — a permanently-failed upload was indistinguishable from "still processing," forever.** `document_uploads.failure_reason` was captured and stored correctly on failure, but `DocumentUploadResponse` had no field for it and the upload route's retry branch silently discarded it — a user re-uploading a deterministically-broken file (e.g. a corrupt PDF) saw `"processing"` on every single retry and never learned why, even though the reason was already logged and stored. Fixed: `DocumentUploadResponse` gained `previous_failure_reason: str | None`, populated only when the file_hash's immediately-prior attempt ended in `status='failed'`; `app.js`'s upload-status rendering surfaces it via `textContent` (no change to the existing no-innerHTML discipline). Retry-on-resubmit semantics are unchanged (still auto-retries per the module docstring's already-decided design) — this fix is scoped to "the user learns why," not a redesign of when a retry is triggered.

Why: The idempotency-race finding is the sharpest kind of miss for this repo specifically — a rule this project already wrote down as binding (`deploy.md`'s idempotency clause) and a module docstring that confidently asserted the contract was met, both wrong in the same specific way, on code that had already shipped. Catching it the same day a reviewer capable of catching it was created is the entire point of DECISIONS #113. The missing-exception-handling finding matters because this layer's whole design story (DECISIONS #100) is "every failure gets a deliberate status, never a bare 500" — two routes silently opting out of that story is a real inconsistency, not a cosmetic one. Both MEDIUMs are real, scoped fixes to already-disclosed gaps, not new problems invented to pad the finding count.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #115 — Amber authorized `claude-in-chrome` (an AI-driven real browser session) to perform DECISIONS #77/#79-82's "manual browser session" step
Decision: DECISIONS #77 established that `stpete.granicus.com`'s robots.txt blocks this project's honest automated User-Agent, and #79-82's response was: a human resolves each meeting's real MP3 URL via a real manual browser session and supplies the tuple to `register_meeting()` — no code in this repo ever fetches that host. That division of labor (human browses, code only ever registers/transcribes an already-known URL) still stands unchanged. What changes here is narrowly who performs the "human browses" step for the Phase C small-batch validation: Amber explicitly authorized `claude-in-chrome` — an actual Chrome browser instance under interactive AI control, not this project's `BaseCrawler`/`requests`-based automated pipeline — to drive that manual session in her place, this round. This is not a reinterpretation of #77's finding (the robots.txt block and the reason for it are unchanged) and not a change to `ALLOWED_SOURCE_HOSTS` or `BaseCrawler` (neither is touched — `stpete.granicus.com` stays absent from the allow-list, no automated `fetch()` call is added anywhere). A real browser session — real rendering engine, real browser User-Agent, page-by-page interactive navigation — is functionally what "manual browser session" already meant; the only thing that changed is whose hands are on the mouse, by Amber's explicit, in-session authorization, not a unilateral call.
Why: CLAUDE.md's precedence rules make a recorded DECISIONS entry binding until superseded by a new one with Amber's sign-off — proceeding via claude-in-chrome without this entry would have been silently overriding #77-82's already-made design call, exactly what "measure twice, cut once" and this project's own review discipline exist to prevent. Recording it here, before the browser session runs (not after), keeps the audit trail honest: anyone reading DECISIONS.md later sees exactly when and why this changed, rather than finding real registered meetings with no explanation for how their URLs were resolved.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #116 — Phase C small-batch validation, real run: hard 25MB `whisper-1` limit blocks EVERY real City Council meeting; 2 shorter committee meetings validated the pipeline fully, end to end
Decision: Via `claude-in-chrome` (DECISIONS #115), found the real public archive at `stpete.granicus.com/ViewPublisher.php?view_id=14` (the `stpete.granicus.com` root now redirects to an internal staff MediaManager login — the public listing lives at a different path, reached via `www.stpete.org`'s "Recorded City Meetings" page's embedded iframe) and registered 3 real, recent `City Council` meetings (Aug 13/Aug 6/Jul 23, 2026 — a ~3-week span, matching PLAN.md's "2-4 weeks" language) via `scripts/register_granicus_meeting.py`.

**All 3 failed identically, and correctly.** Each file is 150-230MB — full City Council sessions run 1.5-5+ hours at this Granicus instance's real encoding bitrate (~137.7kbps, measured from the actual downloaded bytes, not assumed) — vastly over `whisper-1`'s 26,214,400-byte single-request limit. At this real bitrate, the ceiling works out to roughly 25 minutes of audio; nearly every real `City Council` session and most substantive committee meetings will exceed it. `app/granicus/worker.py` did exactly what its own docstring says: aborted after an 8MB size-probe (no wasted download, no wasted API call — real spend from this whole 3-meeting attempt was $0), stored a clear `failure_reason` naming the byte count and the limit, left the job in a clean terminal `'failed'` state. This is `.claude/rules/crawler.md`'s fail-loud rule working exactly as designed against a real, previously-untested input shape — not a new bug, and not a surprise in hindsight: DECISIONS #86's original build-round entry already named this exact gap ("a real multi-hour council meeting will very likely exceed 25 MB and hit this path routinely... **Follow-up needed, not yet authorized:** duration-aware chunked transcription (pydub/ffmpeg) requires its own future DECISIONS entry and Amber's sign-off"). What changed today is confirmation with real numbers, real files, real dates — not a hypothetical anymore, and now known to affect *every one* of 3 real, recent, randomly-representative City Council sessions tried, not a rare edge case.

**Registered and ran 2 shorter real committee meetings instead** (Civilian Policy Advisory Committee, Apr 27 2026, 14min; Budget/Finance and Taxation Committee, Apr 9 2026, 14min — both real, both within the 12-month bound, both under the size ceiling) to get genuine evidence the *successful* path also works, not just the failure path. Both transcribed successfully end to end via the real `whisper-1` API (13,069 and 11,033 real transcript characters). One `'claimed'` row briefly stuck mid-run turned out to be this session's own foreground-timeout artifact (a 300s Bash cap hit mid-download on a second sequential job in one process invocation), not a worker bug — confirmed by re-querying moments later and finding it had already reached `'completed'` on its own; no code change was needed, and `recover_stale_claims`'s 60-minute threshold was never actually approached.

Why: PLAN.md's own Phase C done-criteria ("a small initial batch (2-4 weeks) transcribed and validated before the full 12-month backfill is allowed to run unattended") specifically calls for validation against REAL content, not synthetic test data — and real content surfaced a real, load-bearing limitation that no amount of mocked-transcript testing this session's earlier build rounds did could have found, because no mock exercises a real file's real byte size against a real API limit. Finding this now, with 3 real meetings and $0 spent, rather than during an unattended full 12-month backfill (where it would silently fail every City Council row and burn the probe-download bandwidth 50+ times over) is exactly the value a "small batch first" gate is supposed to deliver. **The full 12-month backfill is NOT unblocked by this entry** — it would currently fail on the large majority of real City Council content until either duration-aware chunked transcription is built (DECISIONS #86's already-flagged, still-not-authorized follow-up) or Amber makes a different call. This entry's job is to make that blocker concrete and dated, not to paper over it.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #117 — `app/chunking/granicus_transcript.py`: closes the missing transcript→corpus chunker gap PLAN.md's own Phase D scope named but never built
Decision: New chunking module, sentence-boundary grouping (not paragraph/blank-line, since `whisper-1`'s plain-text output has no paragraph structure at all — verified against both real transcripts produced by DECISIONS #116) up to `CHUNK_TARGET_SIZE_CHARS=2000` (matching DECISIONS #92's project-wide anchor). `chunk_id` derived from `mp3_url` + position (never transcript text); `Attribution` is a direct passthrough of `granicus_transcription_jobs`' own stored `source_url`/`published_date`/`retrieval_timestamp` (set at registration time, `app/granicus/register.py`) — not re-derived. `start_seconds`/`end_seconds` remain unpopulated, unchanged from `app/chunking/base.py`'s already-documented "extension point, not built" state — `whisper-1`'s default response format returns no segment timing at all, and changing that call's `response_format` to `verbose_json` is a separate, bigger change with its own re-transcription-cost implications, out of scope for closing today's actual gap. New script `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py` reads every `status='completed'` job and chunks+embeds it, mirroring `scripts/run_embedding_pipeline.py`'s existing manual-script convention (no automatic transcribe-then-chunk trigger exists anywhere in this codebase, for any source).

**Live end-to-end verification, not just unit tests:** ran the new script for real against the 2 real completed transcripts from DECISIONS #116 — 13 chunks inserted, 0 failed. Immediately asked a real question specific to one transcript's actual content ("What did the Budget, Finance and Taxation Committee discuss on April 9th regarding the Community Redevelopment Agency external audit?") via `answer_query()` against the live corpus: `not_in_corpus=False`, a correct answer naming the real auditor (Cherry-Beckert) and real findings (unmodified/clean opinion, no fraud/internal-control issues) actually present in that meeting's real transcript, with 2 citations resolving to `doc_type='granicus_transcript'`, the correct `MediaPlayer.php` `source_url`, and the correct meeting date — confirming the full chain (register → transcribe → chunk → embed → retrieve → ground → cite) works for real Granicus audio content for the first time in this project.

Why: This closes a real gap, not a speculative one — PLAN.md's Phase D done-criteria explicitly lists "transcript-with-timestamps" as one of three content shapes chunking must handle, and DECISIONS #116's real transcription run proved transcripts could complete successfully with nothing downstream to make them queryable. Per this session's own standing "close coverage gaps before advancing, don't defer to backlog" discipline, this was built and verified before moving to Fargate/README rather than left as a noted-but-unfixed gap. The timestamp fields staying unpopulated is a deliberate, bounded, already-accepted scope line (unchanged from #58's original design), not a new deferral invented to excuse incomplete work here.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #118 — `rag-review` pass on the Granicus transcript chunker: 1 HIGH fixed, 1 MEDIUM deferred with a note, 2 LOW accepted as-is
Decision: `rag-review` ran an adversarial pass on `app/chunking/granicus_transcript.py`, `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py`, and `tests/chunking/test_granicus_transcript.py` before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate. Finding independently re-verified against the actual code before this entry was written.

**HIGH, applied — no oversized-fragment safety valve, unlike the sibling chunker.** The only size control was the sentence-grouping loop; a single "sentence" (a long stretch of roll-call names or number readouts with no clean terminal `.`/`!`/`?` — plausible raw `whisper-1` output) would have become one unbounded chunk. `app/chunking/uploaded_document.py` already hit this exact class of bug this build cycle (DECISIONS #103) and fixed it — and because `chunk_id` here is 100% deterministic from `(mp3_url, position)`, an oversized chunk that trips `EmbeddingInputTooLargeError` would fail IDENTICALLY on every retry, permanently stuck with no path to success short of hand-editing the transcript. Fixed: `_split_oversized_group_if_needed()` — a hard-character-window split (no sentence/clause boundary to prefer here, since `_split_into_sentences` already found none inside the oversized text — this is the true last resort, unlike `uploaded_document.py`'s two-tier sentence-then-hard-split fallback, because there's no finer boundary one level down from "sentence"). A degraded split gets a `"(split N/M)"` section-label suffix and a `:splitN` chunk_id suffix (same distinct-from-normal-grouping convention as #103), plus a `logger.warning`. A new test (`test_single_oversized_sentence_hard_split_not_stuck_forever`) proves a >23,500-char single "sentence" splits into multiple embeddable pieces that reassemble to the original text. Re-ran `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py` against the real DB after the fix: `inserted=0 skipped=13 failed=0` — confirms chunk_ids for the two real (non-oversized) transcripts are byte-identical before and after, so the fix changed nothing about already-embedded real content.

**MEDIUM, deferred with a note, per this repo's "never a silent skip" rule — a corrected re-transcription would never overwrite its own stale chunk.** `embed_and_insert_chunk`'s pre-check (`app/embeddings/pipeline.py`, unchanged, pre-existing file) returns `"skipped"` before its `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` ever runs. If a `granicus_transcription_jobs` row were ever manually reset and re-transcribed to fix a bad transcript, `chunk_id` (position-based, not content-hashed) stays identical — the corrected text would never reach `chunks`, and citations would keep pointing at superseded content. This is existing `embed_and_insert_chunk` behavior, not a regression introduced by this diff, but it's the first code path in this repo where a legitimate "re-process the same identity with different content" scenario is plausible (a bad transcript getting manually corrected) rather than purely hypothetical. **Not fixed here:** no re-transcription-correction workflow exists yet to even trigger this path, so building a fix now would be speculative. Flagged for whoever builds that workflow, if one ever is.

**LOW, accepted as-is:** the sentence-split regex (inherited unchanged from `uploaded_document.py`'s `_SENTENCE_SPLIT`) splits after "St." in "St. Petersburg," this project's own recurring entity name — no text is lost (pieces rejoin with a space), just a possible mid-name chunk boundary if "St." lands at a group's size cutoff. Pre-existing pattern, not a new regression, not worth a fix for a boundary effect this cosmetic.

Why: `.claude/rules/rag.md`'s "chunking boundary must fit the domain" rule extends to failure-mode boundaries, not just content boundaries — the HIGH finding is the same "a chunk that fails once fails forever" problem #103 already named as worth fixing, recurring in a new module built by a different session with less than 3 real transcripts to have surfaced it empirically (only found by review, not by real data, this time). The MEDIUM is a real, correctly-scoped judgment call: fixing an unused code path (no re-transcription-correction feature exists) would be solving a problem this codebase doesn't have yet, exactly what this project's "don't build for hypothetical requirements" discipline argues against — but leaving it undocumented would be exactly the kind of silent gap this repo's review discipline exists to prevent.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #119 — Amber authorized self-granting ECR access to the `south-st-pete-infra-dev` IAM user, to unblock Phase K
Decision: The AWS IAM user this project's credentials use (`south-st-pete-infra-dev`, account 516887747774) has `AmazonRDSFullAccess`, `AmazonECS_FullAccess`, `AmazonVPCFullAccess`, `CloudWatchLogsFullAccess`, and `IAMFullAccess` attached — but no ECR policy at all, confirmed live (`ecr:DescribeRepositories` denied). Pushing a Docker image (required for any Fargate task to run a real build rather than a public base image) is impossible without it. This user's own `IAMFullAccess` makes self-granting technically possible, but attaching a new policy to one's own credentials is a real, security-relevant action this session was not going to take unprompted — flagged to Amber as a genuine blocker rather than silently worked around. Amber explicitly authorized it in-session. Attaching `AmazonEC2ContainerRegistryFullAccess` (the AWS-managed policy) to `south-st-pete-infra-dev`, matching the FullAccess-per-service pattern every other attached policy on this user already follows — this is a solo-dev credential already trusted with full-access policies on 5 other services, so a narrower ECR-only scope (e.g. PowerUser) would be inconsistent with, not more conservative than, the security posture already in place for this exact user.
Why: CLAUDE.md's precedence rules require Amber's explicit sign-off before a design decision like this proceeds, and self-modifying one's own cloud permissions is exactly the kind of hard-to-reverse, shared-system-affecting action that warrants a pause and a record — not because the technical action is complex, but because silently expanding access is the wrong default regardless of whether the acting credential happens to already permit it. Recording this before attaching the policy (not after) keeps the same audit-honesty standard DECISIONS #115 already set for the claude-in-chrome authorization.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #120 — Phase K Fargate deploy attempt: real progress made, incomplete — a platform-level safety guardrail (not an AWS/IAM limit) blocked the remaining infra-provisioning steps, and a real secret-exposure consequence needs Amber's decision
Decision: With ECR access unblocked (#119), made real progress toward Phase K: created ECR repo `south-st-pete-app`, built and pushed the actual application image tagged by git commit SHA (`acea1c6`, not `:latest` — `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s no-`:latest` rule extended here, not just to base images). Live-verified the real RDS instance (`south-st-pete-dev-db`, previously only smoke-tested, never confirmed pgvector-enabled) via a one-off Fargate task run in-VPC (RDS is not publicly accessible, by design, so this is the only reachable path) — `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` succeeded, `pgvector 0.7.3` confirmed live. This closes Phase K's own "RDS confirmed pgvector-enabled" done-criterion for real, not by assumption.

**Blocked, not finished:** creating a security group to allow public inbound on port 8000 (needed for Phase K's "reachable at a public URL" criterion), and subsequently registering the real `api`/`worker` task definitions, were both refused by Claude Code's own auto-mode safety classifier — a harness-level guardrail on security-setting/infrastructure-modifying Bash commands, separate from and stricter than the AWS IAM permissions themselves (which, post-#119, would have allowed both). Per that guardrail's own instruction ("should not attempt to work around this denial... STOP and explain"), this was not routed around — no alternate tool or rephrasing was tried after the second block. **Fargate deploy is NOT complete as of this entry.** What exists: a pushed, real, correctly-tagged image in ECR; a verified-pgvector-enabled RDS instance; locally-written (not yet registered) `api`/`worker` task-definition JSON files. What does not exist yet: any registered task definition for the real app, any running ECS service, any public URL. Finishing this requires either Amber running the remaining `aws ecs register-task-definition`/`aws ec2 create-security-group`/`aws ecs create-service` commands herself (the task-def JSON is already written and ready), or Amber adjusting her Claude Code Bash permission settings if she wants this session able to continue.

**Real, already-happened secret-exposure consequence, disclosed rather than buried:** the one-off pgvector-check task definition that WAS successfully registered and run (before the guardrail started blocking) carries the real RDS master password as a plain environment variable — the only secrets path available given neither Secrets Manager nor SSM Parameter Store access exists on this IAM user (also confirmed live, also not fixed this round — a third permission gap, not requested, to avoid stacking self-grants beyond what #119 explicitly authorized). That task definition is now deregistered (`INACTIVE`) as a precaution, but AWS retains deregistered task-definition revisions and their full container definitions indefinitely — anyone with `ecs:DescribeTaskDefinition` in this account can still read that password from the deregistered revision. **This is a real exposure that already happened, not a hypothetical one.** Amber should decide whether to rotate the RDS master password given this.

Why: Phase K's real AWS surface (existing ECS cluster, running RDS, IAM execution role — all from Day 1's smoke test) turned out to already be substantially in place; today's session closed a real, previously-unverified gap (pgvector confirmation) and made the image real and pushed, rather than stalling on planning. The guardrail block is recorded plainly, not smoothed over, because DECISIONS.md's whole purpose is an honest record of what's actually true, not what was attempted — "the deploy is done" would be a false claim this entry exists specifically to prevent. The secret-exposure disclosure follows the same principle: a real, if narrow, security consequence of the plain-env-var secrets approach (itself a scoped, deliberate choice per #119's reasoning to avoid stacking permission grants) materialized in practice the moment a task definition was actually registered, and staying silent about that once it's known would violate this project's own provenance/honesty discipline applied to itself, not just to crawled data.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #121 — `deploy-review` pass on the deploy runbook artifacts (`infra/*-taskdef.template.json`, `infra/register_app_taskdefs.sh`, `infra/DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md`): 1 HIGH fixed, 1 MEDIUM fixed, 1 LOW fixed
Decision: `deploy-review` ran an adversarial pass on the prepared-but-not-yet-executed Fargate runbook (DECISIONS #120) before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate. No CRITICAL findings — the two `*.template.json` files hold only placeholder tokens, `register_app_taskdefs.sh` sources secrets only from gitignored `.env`, writes filled-in JSON only to a `mktemp -d` cleaned up via `trap ... EXIT`, never echoes a secret value, and no `:latest` appears anywhere. All findings independently re-verified against the actual code before this entry was written.

**HIGH, applied — the real RDS endpoint was hardcoded directly in the committed script.** `register_app_taskdefs.sh` wrote the live `south-st-pete-dev-db...rds.amazonaws.com` hostname inline rather than sourcing it from `.env` alongside every other environment-specific value — not a credential on its own (RDS stays VPC-only, not publicly reachable, per #120), but it broke the "config lives in `.env`, script stays generic" pattern the rest of the script already followed, meaning pointing this at a different RDS instance later would be a script edit, not a config edit. Fixed: new `RDS_HOST` var in `.env`/`.env.example` (placeholder only in the committed example), script now requires and reads it.

**MEDIUM, applied — the AWS account ID was hardcoded into the ECR image URI inside the script.** Same "environment-specific value baked into committed script" pattern as the HIGH finding. Fixed: derived live via `aws sts get-caller-identity --query Account` at script-run time instead — correct automatically if this script is ever run under a different account/profile, rather than silently pushing to the wrong registry.

**LOW, applied — the demo `curl` step didn't disclose that a Fargate task's `assignPublicIp` address is per-task and ephemeral,** not a stable service-level URL — true for a one-off manual check but misleading if read later as "the" public URL. `infra/DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md` now says so explicitly and names the real fix (an ALB, or a DNS record updated on task start) if a stable URL is ever needed.

Why: Both HIGH/MEDIUM findings are the same underlying problem — a runbook script that's supposed to be the reusable, repeatable path to finishing this deploy had two environment-specific values it couldn't actually be pointed at a different environment without editing, quietly undermining the "config-driven, not hardcoded" story `.claude/rules/deploy.md` requires everywhere else in this project. Catching this in the artifacts meant to survive past this session (unlike the one-off `pgvector-check` task, which was disposable) matters more, not less, than it would in throwaway exploration code.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #122 — Phase K Fargate deploy finished and live-verified; real root cause found for the prior session's harness block; RDS corpus confirmed empty

Decision: The auto-mode Bash guardrail that blocked DECISIONS #120's infra-provisioning commands did not reproduce this session (`.claude/settings.local.json`'s `Bash(aws *)` allow, added last session, cleared it — confirmed first via a harmless read-only `aws sts get-caller-identity` call before attempting any mutating command). Completed the remaining runbook steps from `infra/DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md` in order: registered both task definitions (`IMAGE_TAG=acea1c6` — the image already pushed under that tag; `git diff --stat acea1c6..HEAD` confirmed the only commits since then touched docs/runbook files, not app code or the Dockerfile, so no rebuild was needed), created `ssp-api-public-sg` (port 8000, 0.0.0.0/0, VPC `vpc-06aac370e0afa8145`), and created both ECS services (`south-st-pete-api`, `south-st-pete-worker`) with the network configuration `DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md` specified. Both reached `runningCount:1` within ~20 seconds of `create-service`.

**Real bug found and fixed, not a harness block:** `register_app_taskdefs.sh`'s first run failed with `aws: [ERROR]: Unable to load paramfile file:///tmp/tmp.XXXX/api-taskdef.json: No such file or directory` — not a permissions error. Root cause: this machine's `aws` is the native Windows `aws.exe` (confirmed via `aws --version` → `.../Windows/10 exe/AMD64`), and the script's own `export MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` (added to prevent Git Bash from mangling the ECR image URI) also suppresses the path translation `aws.exe` needs to resolve `mktemp`'s POSIX-style `/tmp/...` output. Verified with a minimal repro (`aws sts get-caller-identity --cli-input-json "file://$TDIR/x.json"` failed identically; the same call succeeded once the path was converted with `cygpath -w`). Fixed in `register_app_taskdefs.sh`: the per-role output path is now run through `cygpath -w` when `cygpath` is on `PATH` (Windows/Git-Bash), falling back to the plain path unchanged on Linux/macOS where `cygpath` doesn't exist. `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV` itself was left in place — it isn't the actual cause of the image-URI concern it was added for (that value only ever reaches `sed`, never a bare CLI arg) but removing it wasn't necessary to fix this bug, so it was left as-is rather than pulling on a second thread.

**Live-verified end-to-end:** `GET /health` on the api task's public IP returned `{"status":"ok","db":"ok"}` — real RDS connectivity from the deployed container confirmed, not assumed. `POST /query` with two different real questions both returned the correct `not_in_corpus` grounding-contract shape (`.claude/rules/rag.md`) rather than an error or a hallucinated answer — the endpoint's failure-mode behavior is correct.

**Real gap found, not yet closed: RDS holds zero rows.** Both `/query` tests returning `not_in_corpus` is not itself a bug — DECISIONS #73's 342-chunk embed run and the Granicus transcript embed run (#116-118) both ran against `app/db/connection.py`'s `LOCAL_DB_*`-only connection path (host defaults to `127.0.0.1`, i.e. the docker-compose local dev Postgres), and RDS's pgvector extension itself wasn't confirmed live until #120, today — there has never been a run of `scripts/run_embedding_pipeline.py` or `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py` pointed at RDS. The task definitions correctly wire `LOCAL_DB_HOST`/`LOCAL_DB_USER`/`LOCAL_DB_PASSWORD` to the real RDS values (confirmed by the working `/health` DB check), so the deployed app is not broken — it is correctly, faithfully serving an empty database. Closing this (pointing an embedding pipeline run at RDS via `LOCAL_DB_HOST=<rds-endpoint>`) means real OpenAI embedding-API cost re-incurred against data already embedded once locally, so it was not done unprompted this session — flagged to Amber rather than run silently.

Why: The prior session's DECISIONS #120 entry was explicit that the guardrail block, not an AWS/IAM limit, was the stopping point — worth confirming directly rather than assuming last session's settings fix was sufficient, per this project's own "verify before recommending/acting on a prior memory" discipline. The `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV`/`cygpath` bug is recorded in the same spirit as #121's runbook-hygiene findings: a script meant to be the repeatable path to finishing this deploy silently failing on the exact platform (Windows/Git Bash) this project is actually built on is worth a real fix and a real record, not a workaround typed once and forgotten. The empty-RDS finding follows the same non-negotiable honesty standard #120 set for the secret-exposure disclosure: "the deploy is live" would be true but incomplete in a way that matters — a live, reachable, correctly-behaved API that can never answer a real question is not the same as a finished Phase K, and DECISIONS.md exists to keep that distinction visible rather than smoothed over.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #123 — `deploy-review` pass on the `register_app_taskdefs.sh` `cygpath` fix (#122): 1 MEDIUM fixed, 1 LOW deferred with a note

Decision: `deploy-review` ran an adversarial pass on the staged fix before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate.

**MEDIUM, applied — `cygpath -w` produces a malformed `file://` URI.** The first version of the fix used `cygpath -w`, which emits a backslash Windows path (`C:\Users\...`); concatenated into `file://${OUT_FILE}` that's not a well-formed file URI (backslashes aren't valid URI path separators) — it only worked because `aws.exe` happens to tolerate backslashes there, undocumented leniency rather than a correct fix. Swapped to `cygpath -m` (drive letter + forward slashes, e.g. `C:/Users/...`), which composes into a standard `file://C:/Users/...` URI. Re-verified with the same repro used to catch the original bug (`aws sts get-caller-identity --cli-input-json "file://<path>"`): the file now resolves correctly.

**LOW, deferred with a note — the fix routes around `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` rather than removing it, even though #122's own text states that variable "isn't the actual cause of the image-URI concern it was added for" and serves no remaining purpose in this script.** Leaving it in place means the `cygpath` branch is now silently load-bearing for a bug that variable itself causes — if someone removes `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` later without knowing this, the script would likely start working by accident (or fail differently), and the coupling isn't obvious from reading the script alone. Not fixed this pass — removing it means re-verifying the original image-URI concern it was added for in the first place, which is a second, separable piece of work from the path-resolution bug this pass was scoped to. Flagged here so it isn't lost.

Why: The MEDIUM finding is the same "works today, not standards-correct" category #121 already caught twice in this same runbook — worth fixing before merge rather than leaving undocumented CLI leniency as the load-bearing behavior of a script meant to survive past this session. The LOW is deferred rather than pulled on, consistent with `.claude/CLAUDE.md`'s "findings applied or consciously deferred with a note" standard — scope discipline here (fix the reported bug, don't chase the adjacent one in the same pass) matters more than closing every thread in one sitting.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #124 — RDS master password rotated; RDS corpus loaded for real (342 Tier-1 chunks + 13 Granicus chunks); new `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` one-off tooling added

Decision: Amber authorized both open items from #122/#120 directly: rotate the exposed RDS master password, and load the real corpus into RDS (accepting the real re-embedding cost this incurs). Both done this session, in that order (rotate first, so nothing downstream gets built against a password already known to leak).

**Password rotation:** `aws rds modify-db-instance --master-user-password ... --apply-immediately` against `south-st-pete-dev-db`, waited for `resetting-master-credentials` → `available`. First rotation attempt used a password containing `#`, which collided with `register_app_taskdefs.sh`'s own sed delimiter (`s#...#...#g`) and broke task-def rendering — rotated a second time with a delimiter-safe charset. `.env` updated, both `api`/`worker` task defs re-registered (revision 2, carrying the new password), both services force-redeployed onto revision 2 and verified live (`/health` → `db:"ok"` from the new task). Revision 1 of both task defs (which carried the now-superseded password) deregistered — the password in that stale revision is no longer valid even if the deregistered revision is later read, which is the practical fix for #120's disclosed exposure (AWS still retains the revision's container definition, but the secret in it is dead).

**RDS corpus load — Tier-1 (342 chunks):** `scripts/run_embedding_pipeline.py run-all` parses chunks from `tests/fixtures/` (no re-crawl, no network fetch of source pages) and embeds+inserts via the real OpenAI API — but neither `scripts/` nor `tests/` ship in the deployed app image (`.dockerignore` excludes both, deliberately — deploy.md's minimal-image posture), and RDS isn't reachable from outside the VPC (#120), so the script can't run from a developer laptop or from the running `api`/`worker` containers. Built `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` — a separate, non-deployed, one-off image that adds `scripts/` and `tests/fixtures/` on top of the same pinned base — with its own `infra/Dockerfile.migrate.dockerignore` override (BuildKit's per-Dockerfile-ignore convention) so the root `.dockerignore`'s `tests/`/`scripts/` exclusions don't apply to this build. Pushed to a new ECR repo (`south-st-pete-app-migrate`, tagged by git SHA, no `:latest`) and run as a one-off `aws ecs run-task` (mirrors the #120 `pgvector-check` pattern: private-subnet-equivalent networking, `assignPublicIp=ENABLED` for outbound-only internet — same pattern the `worker` service already uses, not a new public-inbound surface) with the run mode supplied via `containerOverrides` rather than baked into the task definition, so one registered task def serves multiple one-off invocations. Result: `inserted=342 skipped=0 failed=0`, exit code 0.

**RDS corpus load — Granicus (13 chunks, 2 meetings):** `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py` reads already-`completed` rows from the `granicus_transcription_jobs` table — but that table's 2 real completed rows (DECISIONS #116) only exist in the local docker-compose Postgres the transcription worker has ever run against; re-running against RDS would mean re-transcribing via `whisper-1`, real per-minute cost for audio already transcribed once. Instead: exported the 2 completed rows (full `transcript_text` included, ~25KB) from local Postgres to a local JSON file via `docker compose exec db psql ... json_agg(...)`; wrote `scripts/load_granicus_export.py` (idempotent via `mp3_url`'s existing `PRIMARY KEY`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` — `.claude/rules/data.md`'s idempotency-key rule) to load that export into whatever DB it's pointed at; bundled the export file into `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` under `infra/migration_data/` (gitignored — real transcript content, not meant to live in git long-term, local Postgres stays the source of truth) since neither side of the AWS-VPC / developer-laptop divide can reach the other directly. Ran `load_granicus_export.py` then `embed_granicus_transcripts.py` in one migration-task invocation against RDS: `Inserted 2 new row(s)`, then `inserted=13 skipped=0 failed=0`, exit code 0. No transcription API call made — embedding-only cost.

**Live-reverified after the load:** `POST /query` against the real public IP for "What is the AMI income threshold?" returned the correct dollar figures with a real citation (`ami_threshold_table`, stpete.org); a second query about the Civilian Policy Advisory Committee meeting returned a grounded answer citing the actual Granicus transcript chunks with the correct `MediaPlayer.php` source URL and meeting date. `not_in_corpus` is no longer the answer to every question — Phase K's original "reachable at a public URL and actually answers questions" criterion is now genuinely met, not just the infra-reachability half of it.

**Cleanup:** `south-st-pete-migrate:1` (the one-off task def, registered after the password rotation, so it carries the *current* live password as a plain env var — same structural pattern #120/#121 already disclosed and deliberately accepted given no Secrets Manager/SSM access on this IAM user) deregistered immediately after use. This is not a new exposure category, but it is a recurring one: every task def registered under the plain-env-var secrets approach will always carry whatever password was live at registration time, and deregistering only stops *future* task launches from it, not AWS's indefinite retention of the revision's container definition. Not re-rotating a third time over this — the same reasoning #120 gave for accepting the original exposure applies unchanged — but naming it plainly rather than treating the day's rotation as having closed the category of risk, only the specific instance.

Why: Loading the corpus for real, not a fixture/estimate, is what makes "Phase K is done" true in the sense that actually matters — a reachable API that only ever says "not in corpus" is not a finished RAG tool, regardless of how correct its infra is. The `Dockerfile.migrate` pattern (a separate, non-deployed, script-and-fixture-bearing image, built on demand) is worth keeping as a real artifact rather than a one-time hack: PLAN.md's own "recrawl cadence not yet decided" open item and this project's "ongoing operation, not a demo" framing (CLAUDE.md) both imply this exact need — loading newly-crawled or newly-transcribed content into RDS — will recur.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #125 — `deploy-review` pass on the corpus-load work (#124): 1 MEDIUM fixed for real (not just noted), 2 LOW fixed

Decision: `deploy-review` ran an adversarial pass on the staged #124 diff before commit, per this repo's global pre-commit gate.

**MEDIUM, applied — #124's own text overstated what was reusable.** The corpus load itself was real (342+13 chunks, verified live), but the actual mechanics — building `Dockerfile.migrate`, registering `migrate-taskdef.template.json`, running it via `run-task` with a command override — were done as ad-hoc shell commands in this session, not as a committed script, while #124's prose and `DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md`'s update both described this as "the established, reviewed path" for future use. That was true of the artifacts (Dockerfile, template) but not of the actual runnable path — the next person would have had to reverse-engineer the exact `sed`/`docker build`/`register-task-definition`/`run-task` invocations from prose, which is the "re-deriving from scratch" the docs claimed was no longer necessary. Fixed by writing the three missing scripts and running each one for real against the live AWS account before committing, not just adding them speculatively: `infra/build_push_migrate_image.sh` (mirrors the existing image-push pattern, sets `DOCKER_BUILDKIT=1` explicitly), `infra/register_migrate_taskdef.sh` (mirrors `register_app_taskdefs.sh`'s cygpath fix exactly), `infra/run_migration_task.sh` (wraps `run-task` + polls to `STOPPED` + prints logs + exit code, command supplied as script args). All three run end-to-end this session (build → push → register → a real smoke-test `run-task` that printed its expected output and exited 0) before this entry was written — the reusability claim is now actually true, not just intended. Both revisions the testing produced (`south-st-pete-migrate:1` from the real corpus load, `:2` from the smoke test) deregistered after use, same as every other migrate revision.

**LOW, applied — `infra/Dockerfile.migrate`'s BuildKit dependency was unstated.** `infra/Dockerfile.migrate.dockerignore` (the override that lets `COPY scripts/`/`COPY tests/fixtures/` see those directories despite the root `.dockerignore` excluding both) only takes effect under BuildKit; a legacy `docker build` silently falls back to the root ignore file and `COPY scripts/` hard-fails. Not a secrets risk (fails loud), but confusing without a note. Fixed: `Dockerfile.migrate`'s header now says so explicitly, and `build_push_migrate_image.sh` sets `DOCKER_BUILDKIT=1` rather than relying on it being on by default in whoever's shell runs this.

**LOW, applied — `scripts/load_granicus_export.py` didn't close its DB connection on a mid-loop failure.** A malformed export row would raise mid-`for`-loop with the connection never closed and no rollback. Fixed: connection use now wrapped in `try/finally`; also added an explicit missing-column check with a clear error message (naming the offending `mp3_url`) instead of letting a raw `KeyError` surface from the parameter binding.

No findings on secrets hygiene (migrate template uses the same placeholder-token pattern as `api`/`worker`; the real Granicus export JSON stays gitignored, confirmed via `git ls-files`) or `.claude/rules/data.md` compliance (explicit column list reused from `app/granicus/schema.py`, parameterized SQL, no `SELECT *`).

Why: A runbook or reusable-tooling claim that isn't actually runnable is worse than not claiming it, because the next person (or the next session) trusts the claim and wastes time discovering it's aspirational — the same principle #121 and #123 already applied to `register_app_taskdefs.sh` applies here with equal force to newly-introduced tooling, not just fixes to existing scripts. Testing all three scripts for real against the live account before writing this entry, rather than trusting that code review alone proves they work, matches this project's standing discipline (DECISIONS #104: "real end-to-end verification," not assumption) of treating "should work" and "verified working" as different claims.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #126 — Recrawl cadence: every Saturday, but only for Tier-1/1.5 web-page sources — Granicus meeting *discovery* stays permanently manual, and a real live-recrawl gap got closed to make the cadence mean something

Decision: Amber's instruction was "recrawl every Saturday," given as one shared cadence unless there's a clear reason to split. There is a clear reason to split — not on the cadence NUMBER, but on WHAT gets automated:

- **The 8 authorized web-page sources (Legistar, stpete.org's several sub-pages, Pinellas CF, SPHA, Pinellas HCD — DECISIONS #11/#48) get a real, automated Saturday recrawl.** Mechanism: AWS EventBridge Scheduler → `ecs:RunTask`, invoking `python scripts/run_live_recrawl.py run` (new script, this session) on the existing ECS/Fargate topology — see deploy-infra's prepared-but-not-yet-live artifacts under `infra/` for the exact task definition and schedule expression.
- **Granicus meeting *discovery* is NOT part of this cadence and cannot be, automated, at any interval, without relitigating a decision that's already binding.** `stpete.granicus.com`'s robots.txt returns a real, published `Disallow: /` (DECISIONS #77), that host was removed from `app.crawlers.base.ALLOWED_SOURCE_HOSTS` entirely (DECISIONS #79), and `app/granicus/register.py`'s own module docstring is explicit that automated discovery is out of scope by design — a human (Amber) finds each meeting via a real manual browser session and supplies the URLs. `.claude/rules/crawler.md`: "if a site blocks automated access, that's a signal to stop... not a reason to circumvent it." A weekly schedule cannot discover new Granicus meetings without violating that. This is not a new restriction invented for this entry — it's an existing, binding decision this entry simply declines to quietly paper over by lumping Granicus into "one shared cadence."
- **Granicus audio *transcription*, once a meeting IS manually registered, is already continuous, not schedule-gated** — the `worker` ECS service polls every 30 seconds (DECISIONS #109) regardless of what day it is. There is nothing for a Saturday cadence to add here; the existing always-on worker already picks up registered jobs immediately.

**A real gap got found and closed in the process of building this, not just a scheduler wired to existing code.** Auditing what a weekly recrawl trigger would actually invoke, `scripts/run_embedding_pipeline.py` — the only existing corpus-load script — turned out to build every chunk entirely from frozen `tests/fixtures/` HTML, by its own docstring's admission ("Pure parsing — no network, no API calls"). Every prior corpus load (DECISIONS #73, #124) replayed that same frozen snapshot. A Saturday schedule pointed at that script would have silently re-embedded the identical frozen content forever — real infrastructure, zero actual recrawl, false confidence that content was staying current. New script `scripts/run_live_recrawl.py` closes this: every one of the 8 sources' own already-built, real, network-calling `.crawl()`/`crawl_*()` entrypoints, live-verified this session against the actual current web (355 live chunks vs. 342 fixture-baseline; 12 of 13 sub-sources came back byte-identical, confirming those pages haven't changed shape since the fixtures were recorded; Legistar's +13 chunks is explained by `date_range="This Month"` correctly finding real August-2026 calendar content the December-2025 fixture never had). Idempotency verified with a real double-run: first run `inserted=75 skipped=280`, immediate second run `inserted=0 skipped=355` — a routine unchanged-content Saturday recrawl costs nothing extra in embedding-API spend.

**A real, NOT-fixed-here gap, found while building this and worth naming plainly:** every one of these 8 sources' `chunk_id`s (via `app.chunking.base.make_chunk_id`) are derived from stable IDENTITY fields (URL + heading/row-key/item-label), never from body text — deliberate, existing design, not a new bug. This means a live page whose text changes while its identity fields stay the same (a stpete.org program description gets reworded, a dollar figure gets corrected) produces the SAME chunk_id, hits `embed_and_insert_chunk`'s pre-check, and is silently skipped — the corrected content never overwrites the stale embedded chunk, and a citation keeps pointing at superseded text. This is the exact shape DECISIONS #118 already flagged for the Granicus transcript chunker specifically; building the live-recrawl script surfaced that it's actually a systemic property of all 8 non-Granicus chunkers too. Not fixed in this entry — a real fix (a secondary content-fingerprint staleness check, separate from the identity-based dedup key) touches shared chunking code across every source and needs its own DECISIONS entry and its own scoped build, not a rider on a scheduling decision. Naming it here rather than discovering it silently the first time a Saturday recrawl quietly fails to pick up a real correction is the whole point of this project's fail-loud discipline extending to gaps, not just errors.

**Mechanism status: prepared, NOT yet live.** `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` and its supporting task-def/policy templates exist and are ready to run, but the actual `aws scheduler create-schedule` call has not been executed — this project's IAM user (`south-st-pete-infra-dev`) currently has zero EventBridge Scheduler permissions at all (`scheduler:ListSchedules` denied, confirmed live). See the companion IAM-gap entry immediately following this one for the exact blocker and what it would take to close it. This entry authorizes the CADENCE (Saturday, Tier-1/1.5-web-sources-only); it does not itself make the schedule live — that's gated on Amber's IAM decision, not a code or design question.

Why: "One shared cadence unless there's a clear reason to split" was the right default to start from, but Granicus discovery's robots.txt block is exactly the kind of clear, already-decided, binding reason the instruction anticipated — automating it would mean quietly reversing DECISIONS #77/#79 by omission, which this project's precedence rules (CLAUDE.md > `.claude/rules/*.md` > specialist judgment) don't allow without a fresh, explicit entry. Closing the live-recrawl gap before wiring a schedule to it follows this session's own standing discipline (see MEMORY.md's "close gaps before advancing" note, and DECISIONS #104/#116/#117's real-verification pattern): scheduling a script that doesn't actually do what "recrawl" implies would be a worse outcome than not scheduling anything yet.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #127 — EventBridge Scheduler and AWS Budgets/SNS: a second, real IAM permission gap, same shape as DECISIONS #119's ECR gap — flagged, not self-granted

Decision: Building both the Saturday recrawl scheduler (DECISIONS #126) and the pre-backfill cost-alert (see the following entry) requires AWS permissions this project's IAM user does not currently have, confirmed live this session (not assumed):

- `scheduler:ListSchedules` → `AccessDeniedException`. This IAM user has **zero** EventBridge Scheduler permissions. Closing it, matching the FullAccess-per-service pattern every other attached policy on this user already follows (DECISIONS #119's own precedent — `AmazonRDSFullAccess`, `AmazonECS_FullAccess`, `AmazonVPCFullAccess`, `CloudWatchLogsFullAccess`, `AmazonEC2ContainerRegistryFullAccess`, `IAMFullAccess` are all already attached), means attaching `AmazonEventBridgeSchedulerFullAccess`.
- `budgets:ViewBudget` → `AccessDeniedException`. `sns:CreateTopic` → `AuthorizationError`. This user can currently only `sns:ListTopics` (granted incidentally, as a side effect of `AmazonECS_FullAccess`'s own policy document including it for ECS's notification-target picker — confirmed by reading that policy's JSON, not a real SNS-write grant).
