AWS ships no single "budgets full access" managed policy; closing this needs `AmazonSNSFullAccess` plus a scoped custom policy for `budgets:ViewBudget`/`budgets:ModifyBudget`/`budgets:CreateBudget` (resource `*`) — see `infra/budgets-iam-policy.json` (prepared this session) for the exact document.

**Not self-granted, on the same reasoning as DECISIONS #119.** This IAM user's own `IAMFullAccess` makes self-granting technically possible, but attaching a new policy to this project's live AWS credentials is a real, security-relevant, hard-to-reverse action — #119's own standard (Amber's explicit, in-session sign-off required first, not a unilateral call regardless of how permissive the acting credential already is) applies exactly as much here. Everything that doesn't require these permissions was built and, where possible, verified for real this session (the recrawl script itself, the chunked-transcription work, the ffmpeg Docker addition); everything gated on these two permission grants was prepared ready-to-run (`infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh`, `infra/register_cost_alerts.sh`, and their supporting IAM policy/trust-policy template JSON) but not executed.

**One narrower action deploy-infra considered separately, distinct from the user-level self-grant question above, and did NOT take:** deploy-infra weighed creating just the scoped EventBridge-execution IAM role (`south-st-pete-scheduler-execution-role` — narrower than the account-wide FullAccess policy attachments above, limited to `ecs:RunTask` + `iam:PassRole` against this project's specific task definition/cluster) as a possibly-lower-risk middle option, since this user's existing `IAMFullAccess` would let it succeed today with no permission gap to hit. Concluded the same sign-off requirement applies regardless of scope — no agent message (including any "you MAY" language in this round's own dispatch brief) constitutes Amber's consent, only Amber's own words or the permission system do — and did not create the role. Both `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` (role + weekly-Saturday schedule) and `infra/register_cost_alerts.sh` (SNS topic + AWS Budget) exist, are syntax-checked (`bash -n`), and are ready to run the moment both the `scheduler:*`/`budgets:*`/`sns:*` permission gaps AND the scoped-role creation are authorized — nothing beyond that was executed.

Amber's decision needed: whether to attach `AmazonEventBridgeSchedulerFullAccess` and (`AmazonSNSFullAccess` + the custom budgets policy in `infra/budgets-iam-policy.json`) to `south-st-pete-infra-dev`, mirroring the ECR precedent, and whether to authorize creating the scoped `south-st-pete-scheduler-execution-role` — or handle this some other way (e.g. running these specific `aws iam attach-user-policy`/`create-policy`/`create-role` calls herself, the same fallback DECISIONS #120 named for the Fargate security-group/service-creation guardrail block).
Why: CLAUDE.md's precedence rules require Amber's sign-off before a security-relevant, hard-to-reverse credential change proceeds, regardless of whether the acting credential happens to already permit it — the same reasoning DECISIONS #119 already established, applied consistently rather than re-litigated permissively just because it's the second time this exact shape of blocker has come up.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #128 — Pre-backfill budget alert: mechanism built (AWS Budget + SNS email), NOT live — dollar figure proposed, not decided, gated on the same #127 IAM blocker

Decision: CLAUDE.md is explicit this must exist "before the unattended full backfill runs" — and per this round's own brief, ideally before even the one small real chunked-transcription validation run (DECISIONS #130, once written) that this session's chunked-transcription work needs. `infra/register_cost_alerts.sh` (this session) creates an SNS topic (`south-st-pete-cost-alerts`) with an email subscription, and an AWS Budget (monthly, account-wide — this is a small solo-dev account with one real cost center, no reason to scope narrower) with notification thresholds tied to that topic. Both the email address and the dollar figure are read from `.env` (`BUDGET_ALERT_EMAIL`, `MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD` — both placeholder `changeme` values in `.env.example`, never a real value hardcoded), not invented by any specialist or by me.

**Three reasoned candidate monthly figures, not a unilateral pick:** $60 / $100 / $150. Built from real cost data already in this codebase — steady-state (Fargate `api`+`worker` 24/7 + RDS + trivial embedding-API spend) lands around $40/month; a month that includes a full Granicus transcription backfill push adds roughly $75-95 more (whisper-1 at $0.006/min against real multi-hour meeting audio, per `app/granicus/worker.py`'s own documented pricing). **Recommended: $100/month** — covers steady-state plus a real active-backfill month without being set so high it would fail to catch a genuine runaway-cost bug (e.g. a retry loop gone wrong, or an accidental non-idempotent re-embed of the whole corpus). This is a recommendation, not a decision — Amber picks the real figure via `.env`'s `MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD` before this script is ever run.

**Blocked on the identical IAM gap DECISIONS #127 already documents** (`budgets:ViewBudget` and `sns:CreateTopic` both confirmed denied live) — not a second, separate gap, the same one. Not self-granted, same reasoning as #127. `infra/register_cost_alerts.sh` is syntax-checked (`bash -n`) and ready to run the moment (a) the IAM gap is closed and (b) Amber sets a real `BUDGET_ALERT_EMAIL`/`MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD` in `.env`.

**Sequencing consequence, named plainly rather than smoothed over:** this means the real budget alert will NOT be live before DECISIONS #130's small validation transcription run, contrary to this round's own stated preference ("set this up early enough that it's live before you validate chunked transcription against a real full-length meeting"). Judged acceptable to proceed anyway because: the validation run's own worst-case cost is small and independently bounded (a single meeting, computed from real known file size/bitrate before the call is made, per #130) — not the unattended, open-ended spend an actual full 12-month backfill represents, which remains explicitly out of scope this round regardless of whether this budget alert is live. Naming the sequencing gap here rather than silently proceeding as if it didn't matter is the honest move; the alternative (blocking a small, already-bounded, already-authorized validation run on an IAM grant Amber hasn't been asked about yet) would trade a small, real gap for a much larger delay on already-authorized work.
Why: A real dollar figure on a real AWS account is exactly the kind of consequential-but-not-obvious number CLAUDE.md and this round's brief both say must come from Amber, not be invented under time pressure — proposing reasoned candidates grounded in this project's own real, already-documented cost data (rather than either inventing a round number or refusing to engage with the question at all) gives her something concrete to confirm or adjust quickly instead of starting from zero.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #129 — Reusing `south-st-pete-migrate` for the recurring weekly recrawl schedule extends the plain-env-var RDS-password exposure to a third always-on task family — real, disclosed tradeoff, not yet authorized

Decision: `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` targets the existing `south-st-pete-migrate` task definition/image (`infra/Dockerfile.migrate`, DECISIONS #124) rather than registering a new task family — it already bundles the whole `scripts/` tree (so `scripts/run_live_recrawl.py` lands in it automatically on the next image rebuild) and already has proven outbound egress to external hosts (OpenAI, the Granicus CDN) under the same security-group/subnet configuration a live web-page recrawl needs. Reasonable reuse on its face.

**But it changes that task family's real operating model in a way worth its own record.** `infra/DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md` has documented, since DECISIONS #124, that `south-st-pete-migrate` gets deregistered promptly after each one-off use specifically because its registered revision carries the live RDS master password as a plain environment variable (no Secrets Manager/SSM access on this IAM user, #120's original accepted tradeoff) — deregistering after use was the mitigation that kept the exposure window short. A recurring Saturday schedule needs this task definition to stay an ACTIVE, registered revision indefinitely, or the next week's run has nothing to invoke — that breaks the "register, run, deregister" pattern this task family was specifically operated under.

This is **not a new category of risk** — the live `south-st-pete-api`/`south-st-pete-worker` ECS services have already carried the same plain-env-var RDS password in a permanently-registered ACTIVE task definition, unconditionally, since DECISIONS #122, and that was already accepted. But it IS a real, concrete extension of that accepted tradeoff to a third always-on task family, and per this project's own "never a silent skip" review-deferral discipline, that extension gets named and recorded rather than waved through as an implementation detail of "reuse the existing image."

**Not yet authorized to actually run.** `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` is blocked on DECISIONS #127's IAM gap regardless, so this doesn't change what's executable today — but even once that gap is closed, running this script is a second, separate decision from "EventBridge Scheduler permissions exist," specifically because of this tradeoff. Flagging both to Amber together rather than treating the IAM unblock as the only remaining gate.
Why: The RDS-password-in-plain-env-var exposure is an accepted, disclosed, already-live pattern on two other task families — extending it to a third without saying so plainly would be the kind of quiet scope-creep-by-implementation-detail this project's DECISIONS discipline exists to prevent, even though the actual incremental risk is arguably small (same account, same rotation lever available if it's ever needed again per #124).
Date: 2026-08-23

## #130 — Duration-aware chunked transcription built, reviewed, and REAL-verified against a real full-length City Council meeting — supersedes DECISIONS #86's "decision (b)"

Decision: Amber's explicit authorization of DECISIONS #86's flagged-but-not-built follow-up is now built. New module `app/granicus/audio_split.py` splits an oversized audio file for `whisper-1` by decoding it (`pydub.AudioSegment`, wrapping `ffmpeg` — deploy-infra's parallel Docker addition, DECISIONS #129's sibling work) and slicing in the DECODED PCM domain, never a raw byte-offset cut of the compressed stream — this resolves DECISIONS #86's original, still-valid frame-corruption objection by construction (decoded-domain cuts are sample-accurate by definition) rather than working around it. `app/granicus/worker.py`'s module docstring now records this as "decision (c)," explicitly kept alongside the original "decision (b)" reasoning rather than overwriting it — the history of why (b) was chosen first and what concretely changed to authorize (c) is itself interview-defensible material.

**Split-sizing: a hybrid strategy, not either pure alternative this round's brief posed.** An initial per-piece duration estimate from the file's own real observed bitrate (`total_bytes*8/total_duration`, targeting `SPLIT_TARGET_FRACTION=0.9` of the 25 MiB ceiling) makes the common case converge in one export per piece — but the actual correctness guarantee is a MANDATORY post-export byte-size verification against the real ceiling, with adaptive halving re-split (bounded at `MAX_RESPLIT_ATTEMPTS=5`, ≈32x size-reduction headroom) for any piece that still comes out oversized. The estimate is an optimization; the verify step is load-bearing regardless of whether the estimate was right.

**Two new tunables, both re-derived/derived against exact real evidence, not round numbers:**
- `STALE_CLAIM_THRESHOLD`: 1 hour → **4 hours**. The original 1-hour derivation explicitly depended on the old design's early-abort-before-Whisper behavior, which decision (c) removes — re-derived from scratch against the exact real byte count of the largest meeting DECISIONS #116 actually registered (229,683,110 bytes, not a rounded "~250MB"), giving a realistic 78-minute worst case and two independent pessimistic cross-checks landing at 178-179 minutes; 4 hours gives real, honestly-reported margin (≈3.1x realistic, ≈35% over the pessimistic cross-checks) rather than an overstated one — an earlier draft of this math claimed more margin than the numbers actually supported and `crawler-review` caught it (see below).
- `MAX_TOTAL_DOWNLOAD_BYTES = 500 MiB`: a new, explicit outer sanity ceiling this round's brief asked for — ~2.3x the real observed maximum meeting size to date, comfortably covering any real St. Pete City Council session seen so far while still catching a genuine outlier (a mis-registered non-audio file, a wrong URL) before attempting an unbounded number of Whisper calls. Reuses `_download_ranged`'s existing early-abort mechanism rather than inventing a second one — `AudioTooLargeError`'s meaning shifted from "over Whisper's own limit" to "over this project's own sane processing ceiling," and the old per-request ceiling (`WHISPER_MAX_FILE_BYTES`, unchanged at 25 MiB) is now the per-piece target `audio_split.py` splits against, not a whole-download abort trigger.

**Stitching:** a plain space join (`_PIECE_STITCH_SEPARATOR`), confirmed compatible with `app/chunking/granicus_transcript.py`'s existing `_PIECE_JOIN`/sentence-boundary chunker before being chosen, not assumed — that chunker only ever splits on sentence punctuation and already has an accepted precedent (its own "St. Petersburg" mid-name split note, DECISIONS #118) for a boundary landing at an imperfect seam; a plain-space stitch introduces nothing new for it to handle.

**Failure handling preserves the fail-loud contract at piece granularity, not just job granularity.** A new `PieceTranscriptionError` names exactly which piece (`N/total`) failed and why when one piece's transcription ultimately fails (after `_transcribe_audio`'s own existing retry/classification is exhausted) — the whole job fails loud with that piece identified in `failure_reason`, never a stitched result silently missing one segment's text. A systemic OpenAI auth/permission failure on ANY piece still propagates unwrapped into `process_one_job`'s existing systemic-vs-per-job handling (DECISIONS #74's/#90's shape) unchanged — reverts the whole job to `pending`, doesn't mark it `failed`, exactly as before.

**`crawler-review` pre-commit pass: 2 HIGH + 2 MEDIUM + 2 LOW, all six applied, none deferred.** HIGH: split-piece temp files could leak on a failure partway through the adaptive-resplit recursion (fixed — cascading best-effort cleanup at every recursion level, `.claude/rules/data.md`). HIGH: two concurrent workers processing two different jobs against the same shared temp dir could collide on identical split-piece filenames (fixed — every exported filename namespaced to one `split_audio_for_whisper` call via `split_id`). MEDIUM: a resplit piece's multiple leaf files could report duplicate `SplitPiece.index` values, making a piece-failure message ambiguous about which physical file actually broke (fixed — indices assigned once, after every leaf piece is known). MEDIUM: an earlier draft of the stale-claim math claimed "comfortably" cleared margin on the pessimistic cross-check when the underlying numbers, before switching to the exact real byte count, gave only ~13% margin (fixed — now uses the real exact figure throughout and reports the actual ≈35% margin honestly). LOW: original math mixed decimal-MB and MiB readings between the download-chunk and piece-count steps (fixed — one real byte count used consistently). LOW: an assumption about `pydub`'s export accepting a `Path` argument directly was unverified in-code (resolved empirically by the real validation run itself — 7/7 real exports succeeded against real `Path` objects in the real container, not by a new isolated unit test).

**Real end-to-end validation — not hypothetical, not a mock.** One of DECISIONS #116's 3 real City Council meetings that failed under the old ceiling (Aug 6 2026, previously `status='failed'`) was manually reset to `pending` and reprocessed for real by the actual `worker` service (rebuilt image, real `ffmpeg` present) against the real `whisper-1` API. Real numbers, independently re-confirmed by the orchestrator against the live local Postgres and the worker's own logs, not taken on the specialist's report alone: **150,348,471 bytes, ~194.2 minutes (~3.24 hours) of audio, ~103 kbps observed bitrate, split into 7 whisper-1-safe pieces, real cost ≈$1.17** (matching the worker's own pre-call log estimate exactly), all 7 pieces transcribed successfully, `status='completed'`, `transcript_text` = 165,751 real characters. `scripts/embed_granicus_transcripts.py` then chunked+embedded it for real: 86 new chunks inserted (0 failed, 13 skipped — the 2 pre-existing short committee-meeting transcripts from #116/#117 untouched), independently confirmed via `SELECT count(*) FROM chunks WHERE doc_type='granicus_transcript'` returning 99 (13 + 86). A real, content-specific question about that meeting's actual real content (a Sky Isle/Veridian loan maturity-date extension) returned `not_in_corpus=False` with the correct grounded answer (maturity extended July 1 2044 → July 1 2049) and 2 citations resolving to the correct `doc_type`, `source_url`, and 2026-08-06 date — the full chain (claim → split → transcribe ×7 → stitch → store → chunk → embed → retrieve → ground → cite) proven for a real, full-length, multi-hour City Council meeting for the first time in this project.

**Full test suite: 490 passed, 0 failed** (up from 381 at DECISIONS #91's last full-suite count referenced in this log; includes 13 new `test_audio_split.py` tests against a fake `AudioSegment` stand-in — real `ffmpeg`/`pydub` decode is not exercised by the unit suite, only by the real docker-compose validation run above, per this project's own "no live network/API calls in unit tests" convention).

Why: This closes DECISIONS #86's explicitly-flagged, explicitly-not-yet-authorized gap the moment Amber authorized it, with the same worked-math rigor and real-evidence grounding this project's stale-claim-threshold and cost-transparency decisions have used from the start — and the crawler-review pass catching two real HIGH bugs (temp-file leaks, filename collisions under concurrency) and a real overstated-margin claim before commit is exactly what a pre-commit adversarial pass is for, not a formality. Validating against a real, previously-failed, real full-length meeting (not a synthetic short clip) is what actually proves the fix works for the case that matters — every real City Council session, not just the short committee meetings already proven end-to-end at #116/#117.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #131 — Real, disclosed secret exposure this session: the OpenAI API key was printed in plaintext into a specialist agent's tool-output transcript — Amber should rotate it

Decision: While validating DECISIONS #130's work, the `crawler` specialist building chunked transcription ran a command intended to print `.env` with secrets filtered out (`cat .env | grep -v PASSWORD`), but `OPENAI_API_KEY` does not contain the string "PASSWORD" — it printed in full, in plaintext, into that session's own tool-output transcript. The specialist caught this itself, self-reported it rather than staying quiet, deleted the one temp file the value also landed in, and switched to sourcing `.env` into shell environment variables without echoing for the remainder of its work. **What is NOT fixed, and cannot be, by anything done after the fact: the key already rendered in that transcript and cannot be un-printed from wherever this environment retains session transcripts.**

This is a different exposure shape than DECISIONS #120's RDS-master-password disclosure (that one lived in an AWS-retained deregistered task-definition revision, reachable only by someone with `ecs:DescribeTaskDefinition` on this specific AWS account) — this is an OpenAI API key, printed into a Claude Code session transcript rather than an AWS-internal record, with a different, less precisely-known retention/access surface.

No action taken unilaterally beyond the specialist's own immediate cleanup (deleting the one temp file) — rotating a live API key this project depends on for its core embedding/generation/transcription path is a real, user-facing action (every in-flight request using the old key would need the new one) that Amber should decide the timing/urgency of, not something silently done on her behalf mid-session.

**Recommended: rotate the OpenAI API key.** Given real cost (transcription/embedding/generation all bill against it) and this project's own standing "never bury a real exposure" discipline (DECISIONS #120's precedent — disclosed plainly rather than smoothed over, even though the underlying technical action to fix it wasn't complex), this should not wait.
Why: This project's own honesty discipline, already applied to itself once for the RDS password (#120) and again for this recrawl-schedule tradeoff (#129), applies with equal force here — a real, if narrow and already-mitigated-as-far-as-possible, secret exposure gets a plain record and a clear recommendation, not a quiet fix-and-move-on treated as if it made the exposure retroactively not have happened.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #132 — DECISIONS #127's IAM gap actually closed and REAL-verified live; a real, undocumented prior-session gap found and named plainly; the uncommitted trust-policy regression investigated empirically and resolved

Decision: Amber gave real, explicit, in-session sign-off on DECISIONS #127-#129 this round. Before touching anything, verified identity (`aws sts get-caller-identity --profile south-st-pete` → account `516887747774`, user `south-st-pete-infra-dev`, matching every prior entry).

**Found, not assumed: most of this action was already live before this session started, untracked in DECISIONS.md.** A pre-execution audit (the "verify before recommending/acting" discipline this project already holds itself to, DECISIONS #122) turned up `AmazonEventBridgeSchedulerFullAccess`, `AmazonSNSFullAccess`, and a custom `south-st-pete-budgets-management` policy (matching `infra/budgets-iam-policy.json`'s document exactly) already attached to `south-st-pete-infra-dev`, and the scoped `south-st-pete-scheduler-execution-role` already existing (`CreateDate: 2026-08-24T03:57:21Z`). CloudTrail confirms this was done by this same IAM user's own credentials via `aws-cli/2.36.25` on Windows, in a burst of `PutRolePolicy`/`UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` calls at `2026-08-24T04:00:36Z`-`04:01:08Z` — i.e., a prior session (not this one, and not recorded in any DECISIONS entry) ran `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh`'s IAM-role section for real, shortly before this session began. **This entry does not claim credit for that prior work** — it's disclosed here because DECISIONS.md's whole purpose is an accurate record of what's actually true, and "I attached these policies" would be false. What this session actually did: re-verified the attachments are correct and effective, and closed what was still genuinely missing (the schedule itself — see #134).

**Re-verified live, not assumed:** `aws scheduler list-schedules` and `aws budgets describe-budgets --account-id 516887747774`, both of which 403'd under DECISIONS #127's original audit, now succeed cleanly. `aws iam list-attached-user-policies --user-name south-st-pete-infra-dev` confirms all three policies present.

**The uncommitted `infra/recrawl-scheduler-trust-policy.template.json` diff, investigated and resolved.** The working tree had an unstaged edit dropping the `ArnEquals: {"aws:SourceArn": "__SCHEDULE_ARN__"}` condition, leaving only `SourceAccount`. Two hypotheses were live: a deliberate fix for a chicken-and-egg problem (schedule ARN unknown before the schedule exists), or a stray/incomplete edit. The chicken-and-egg hypothesis doesn't hold on inspection — EventBridge Scheduler ARNs are fully deterministic (`arn:aws:scheduler:{region}:{account}:schedule/{group}/{name}`, no random component), and `register_recrawl_schedule.sh` itself already computes `SCHEDULE_ARN` before creating anything. Live evidence settled it further: the role's actual live trust policy (created by the prior, undocumented session above) still carried the FULL condition including `ArnEquals` with the resolved schedule ARN — meaning the last real, successful use of this template used the version WITH `ArnEquals`, not the working-tree edit. Restored the committed version (`git checkout -- infra/recrawl-scheduler-trust-policy.template.json`) rather than keeping the regression; no net diff remains since the committed file already had the correct content.

**A real, initially-confusing wrinkle, run down empirically rather than papered over:** re-running `register_recrawl_schedule.sh` with `ArnEquals` restored hit `ValidationException: The execution role you provide must allow AWS EventBridge Scheduler to assume the role` on `CreateSchedule` — twice, ~15s apart, which briefly looked like it might vindicate the working-tree edit after all (i.e., that `ArnEquals` really does break `CreateSchedule`). Tested directly: temporarily updated the live role's trust policy to drop `ArnEquals` (matching the working-tree edit) and retried — this run, after a longer real wait, succeeded, but so did a subsequent re-run with `ArnEquals` restored via the script's own template-driven update (see #134). Conclusion: this is IAM eventual consistency after `iam:UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` (a documented AWS behavior — a freshly-changed trust policy can take tens of seconds to propagate to dependent services' validation checks), not a real incompatibility between `ArnEquals` and `CreateSchedule`. The prior session's own two close-together `PutRolePolicy`/`UpdateAssumeRolePolicy` CloudTrail events (04:00:36Z, 04:01:06Z-04:01:08Z) match this exact same failure-and-retry shape, suggesting they hit the identical propagation delay and may have started editing the template as an untested workaround rather than simply waiting longer. `ArnEquals` is the correct, more-scoped condition (confused-deputy protection recommended for EventBridge Scheduler execution roles) and is what's live now, proven working by the successful `CreateSchedule` call in #134.

**Not touched: DECISIONS #131 (OpenAI API key) remains open.** No `.env` contents were echoed at any point this round — env vars were sourced into the shell without printing, per #131's own prescribed fix. No mention or use of the flagged key.

Why: Closing #127 means the permission gap is actually gone in live AWS state, not that a script ran without erroring — the same "verified, not assumed" standard this project has applied to every deploy claim since #104/#122/#125. Naming the untracked prior-session work plainly, rather than silently absorbing it into this entry's own account, matters because DECISIONS.md is Amber's interview-defensible record of what actually happened and when — attributing someone else's already-done work to this session would be exactly the kind of quiet inaccuracy this project's discipline exists to prevent.
Date: 2026-08-24

## #133 — DECISIONS #128 closed: pre-backfill budget alert is live and REAL-verified ($100/month, 50/90/100% actual-spend thresholds, wired to a confirmed email subscription)

Decision: Ran `infra/register_cost_alerts.sh` for real (`AWS_PROFILE=south-st-pete bash infra/register_cost_alerts.sh`), gated on #132's IAM closure. `.env`'s real values used as documented (`BUDGET_ALERT_EMAIL=blaqcoffeeblaqtea@gmail.com`, `MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD=100` — the project's own #128 recommendation, no new decision needed).

**Most of this, too, was already live before this session started** (same undocumented-prior-session pattern as #132): the SNS topic and the AWS Budget both already existed when first checked, budget `LastUpdatedTime: 2026-08-23T23:53:33-04:00` (≈2026-08-24T03:53Z) — consistent with the same prior, unrecorded session window found in #132. Running the script this session was still real and non-trivial, not a no-op: `sns:create-topic`/`sns:subscribe` re-confirmed idempotently, `sns:set-topic-attributes` re-applied the `AllowBudgetsToPublish` resource policy for real (this call runs unconditionally on every invocation, unlike the budget-create step), and the pre-check correctly detected the existing budget and skipped re-creation rather than erroring.

**Live-verified, not assumed:**
- SNS topic `arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:516887747774:south-st-pete-cost-alerts` exists (`aws sns list-topics`).
- Email subscription exists and — checked directly via `aws sns get-subscription-attributes` — `"PendingConfirmation": "false"`, i.e., **already confirmed**, not pending. This session did not click any confirmation link; the subscription was already confirmed before this session's first check, consistent with it being created in the same prior, undocumented window. Disclosed plainly rather than claimed as this session's own action.
- Budget `south-st-pete-monthly-cost-budget` exists via `aws budgets describe-budget`: `BudgetLimit: {"Amount": "100.0", "Unit": "USD"}`, `TimeUnit: MONTHLY`, `HealthStatus: HEALTHY`, real `CalculatedSpend.ActualSpend: $1.60` this cycle.
- `aws budgets describe-notifications-for-budget` confirms all three thresholds present and correctly typed: `ACTUAL`/`GREATER_THAN` at 50%, 90%, 100% — matching `register_cost_alerts.sh`'s intended shape exactly, all three in `NotificationState: OK`.

Why: CLAUDE.md requires a live budget alert before any unattended full backfill — this closes that requirement for real, with independent AWS-side confirmation of the exact dollar figure, threshold count/type, and topic wiring rather than trusting the script's own exit code alone. The "already confirmed, not this session's click" disclosure follows the same standard #132 set: the record states what this session actually verified, not what it would be convenient to imply it did.
Date: 2026-08-24

## #134 — DECISIONS #129 closed: the weekly Saturday recrawl schedule is live and REAL-verified, targeting `south-st-pete-migrate:3` via the scoped execution role — the accepted tradeoff (permanently-ACTIVE task def carrying the RDS password) is now in effect for real, not just authorized on paper

Decision: Amber explicitly accepted #129's disclosed tradeoff (extending the plain-env-var RDS-password exposure, already live on `api`/`worker` since #122, to a third always-on task family: `south-st-pete-migrate` must now stay permanently registered/ACTIVE rather than deregistered after each one-off use, so the weekly schedule has something to invoke). Ran `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` for real, gated on #132's IAM closure and #132's trust-policy-template fix.

**What was genuinely missing (unlike #132/#133's mostly-already-live findings): the schedule itself.** `aws scheduler list-schedules` returned `{"Schedules": []}` at the start of this session — the prior, undocumented session (per #132) had gotten as far as creating/configuring the scoped role but had not gotten a schedule created, consistent with the `ValidationException` this session also hit and diagnosed as IAM eventual consistency (see #132).

**Execution, in order:** (1) role trust/permissions policies re-applied idempotently (`update-assume-role-policy` + `put-role-policy`, both upserts) via the templates, restored to their committed, `ArnEquals`-including form. (2) `CreateSchedule` failed twice with the IAM-propagation `ValidationException` described in #132; after that delay resolved (confirmed empirically, see #132), a subsequent run of this same script succeeded.

**Live-verified via `aws scheduler get-schedule --name south-st-pete-weekly-recrawl --group-name default`, not assumed:**
- `Arn: arn:aws:scheduler:us-east-1:516887747774:schedule/default/south-st-pete-weekly-recrawl`, `State: ENABLED`.
- `ScheduleExpression: cron(0 6 ? * SAT *)`, `ScheduleExpressionTimezone: UTC` — matches DECISIONS #126's documented weekly-Saturday-06:00-UTC cadence exactly.
- `Target.RoleArn`: the scoped `south-st-pete-scheduler-execution-role` (not a broader role) — its live permissions policy (`aws iam get-role-policy`, re-confirmed this session) scopes `ecs:RunTask` to `arn:aws:ecs:us-east-1:516887747774:task-definition/south-st-pete-migrate:*` conditioned on `ecs:cluster == arn:...:cluster/south-st-pete-dev`, and `iam:PassRole` scoped to only `south-st-pete-ecs-task-execution-role` — the narrower-option analysis #127 originally called for, now actually enforcing that scope in live IAM, not just documented as intent.
- `Target.EcsParameters.TaskDefinitionArn`: `arn:...:task-definition/south-st-pete-migrate:3` — the real, live, latest ACTIVE revision (resolved by the script via a live `describe-task-definition` call at run time, not a guessed/hardcoded revision number).
- `Target.Input`: `{"containerOverrides": [{"name": "south-st-pete-migrate", "command": ["python", "scripts/run_live_recrawl.py", "run"]}]}` — the correct live-recrawl entrypoint, not the migration/embedding scripts.
- Network config matches `.env`'s real `FARGATE_SUBNET_IDS`/`FARGATE_SG_ID`, `AssignPublicIp: ENABLED` (outbound-only, same pattern as the existing `worker` service).

**The accepted tradeoff is now live, not just authorized:** `south-st-pete-migrate:3` remains ACTIVE and registered (not deregistered after this session's use), by design — the schedule needs it to stay invokable every Saturday. Amber's #129 sign-off is what makes this acceptable; nothing new is being decided here, only executed and confirmed.

Why: A schedule that exists in `infra/` as a ready-to-run script isn't the same claim as a schedule that AWS will actually fire — this entry closes the gap between "prepared" (#126/#127/#129's language) and "live," with the exact live resource ARNs, role scope, and cron expression checked against what was actually authorized rather than assumed from the script's own comments. Matches this project's standing "should work" vs. "verified working" distinction (DECISIONS #104/#125/#130).

## #135 — Second real, disclosed secret exposure this session: `grep -n "OPENAI_API_KEY" .env` printed the live key into the orchestrating session's own transcript — new `.claude/rules/secrets.md` added

Decision: While answering "where do I put the new key" during #131's still-pending rotation, a direct `grep -n "OPENAI_API_KEY" .env .env.example` was run to locate the line number to point Amber to. Unlike #131's denylist miss (`grep -v PASSWORD` not matching a key name that doesn't contain "PASSWORD"), this was an allowlist match that still printed the full line — including the value — because no output-shaping flag (`-o`, `-c`) was used. The value is now in this session's transcript, on top of #131's still-unrotated exposure of the same key.

Self-reported immediately, not caught after the fact by review — same "own it plainly" discipline as #131 and #120. No attempt made to retract or overwrite the message; a session transcript isn't a retractable medium, so the only real mitigation is rotation (already recommended and still outstanding from #131 — this doesn't create a second rotation obligation, it reinforces the existing one).

Added `.claude/rules/secrets.md`, referenced from this file's own "Workflow" section, codifying: no `grep`/`cat`/`echo` against a secrets file without an output-shaping flag that excludes the value; use `grep -c`/`grep -o` for existence/location checks; source `.env` into shell env vars (no echo) when a value is actually needed in a command. Binding on every specialist and on direct shell commands in the orchestrating session alike — this exposure happened in the latter, which #131's original fix (specialist-only sourcing discipline) didn't cover.
Why: Two real exposures of the same shape (denylist miss, then allowlist-without-shaping) means the failure mode is "printing a secrets file without redirecting output" in general, not a single fixable command — a rule that's discoverable by every future session (via CLAUDE.md's Workflow section, read at Step 0) is the actual fix; asking future-me to "just be careful" a third time is not.
Date: 2026-08-23

## #136 — Renamed to "Blaq Blob"; project purpose reframed from dual-track (advocacy + job-application evidence) to a community-advocacy tool built for successor handoff, not interview-defense material

Decision: Renamed from "South St. Petersburg Infrastructure Intelligence" to **Blaq Blob**, per Amber's direction — a name that reads as a real, watching, unforgettable presence to whoever it's pointed at, fitting a tool built to hold power accountable in an adversarial CBA negotiation, rather than a portfolio-demo title. This is the project's second rename (first: "cba-rag-assistant" → "South St. Petersburg Infrastructure Intelligence," DECISIONS #9).

**The GitHub repo itself (`amwoo1985/south-st-pete-infrastructure-intelligence`) is deliberately NOT renamed this round** — docs/branding only, keeping the existing clone URL, any bookmarked links, and local git remotes intact. A repo rename can be revisited later as its own decision if wanted.

**Also reframed: the project's real audience.** CLAUDE.md previously described this as dual-purpose — a real tool that "also serves as evidence for a CodeBoxx-partner job application" — and README/PLAN.md carried "interview-defense talking points" written for that audience. Amber confirmed there is no interview to defend this in; the sole real audience going forward is whoever she hands day-to-day operation of this tool to next, in ongoing South St. Petersburg Energy Coalition/CBA advocacy work. CLAUDE.md's job-application clause is removed; its "defensible live in an interview" hard-constraint language is reworded to "explainable in plain language to a non-engineer successor" — same underlying discipline (no undocumented magic), honest about who it's actually for. README and `DEMO.md` are being rewritten accordingly: plain-language successor-handoff framing replaces interview-talking-points framing. PLAN.md's own "Interview-Defense Talking Points" section (Section 5) is left as-is, as a historical record of the original sprint framing — CLAUDE.md's "Plan" pointer now sends readers to README's handoff-notes section instead, so PLAN.md's stale framing doesn't need to be excised to stop misleading anyone. The CV+AI-analysis track named in CLAUDE.md's "Two independent tracks" section is untouched — it was already scoped as separate from, and non-gating on, this project; only the job-application clause tying *this project* to that track is removed.

Why: A name and a document only serve their purpose if they're honest about who's going to read them and why. Continuing to maintain "interview-defense" material for an interview that isn't happening would waste effort and, worse, leave the actual audience — a successor who needs to trust and operate this tool without Amber walking them through it live — without the plain explanation they actually need.
Date: 2026-08-24

## #137 — DECISIONS #130's chunked-transcription fix never actually reached production on either ECS resource that needed it; both gaps found, fixed, and REAL-verified live — two different failure shapes, not the same gap twice

Decision: A live AWS audit found that #130's duration-aware chunked transcription (`app/granicus/audio_split.py`, `app/granicus/worker.py`) — built, reviewed, and validated locally that same day — never made it into either running ECS resource that depends on it.

**`south-st-pete-worker` (the persistent service) was simply stale.** It was running task-definition revision `:2`, image `south-st-pete-app:acea1c6` — pushed at #120, before both `6374cc2` (ffmpeg added to the root `Dockerfile`) and `05f9943` (#130's split logic). Confirmed live by watching it independently claim a real pending job and fail with the literal pre-#130 error text ("...aborted after the first 8,388,608-byte probe chunk... no multi-part transcription built this round"), which only exists in the pre-#130 source. Root cause: nothing rebuilt or redeployed this image since `acea1c6` — every commit since (including #130 itself) sat committed but never built, pushed, or rolled onto the service.

**`south-st-pete-migrate` had the opposite problem: current code, missing system dependency.** Its `:3` revision ran an image built from post-#130 source (tag `05f9943`), but crashed with `FileNotFoundError: ffprobe` on a real oversized meeting file. Root cause, found on inspection: `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` — a separate Dockerfile from the root one — never had ffmpeg installed. The root `Dockerfile` gained it in `6374cc2`; `Dockerfile.migrate` was never given the same line. A real, structural gap in the image definition itself, not a stale-tag problem — rebuilding at any commit since `6374cc2` would have hit the same failure.

**Verified locally before touching AWS, per `.claude/rules/deploy.md`'s discipline of checking rather than assuming committed state is correct:** built the unmodified root `Dockerfile` at then-`HEAD`, then inside it: `which ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` both resolved, `audio_split.SPLIT_TARGET_FRACTION`/`MAX_RESPLIT_ATTEMPTS` both importable, pre-#130 error string absent from `worker`'s source. **Root cause on the root `Dockerfile`: confirmed already correct, exactly as suspected — zero change needed there.** `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` got the same `apt-get install ffmpeg` line the root Dockerfile already carries, same layer position (before pip-install, same cache-ordering rationale), rebuilt and re-verified the same three checks before pushing anything.

**No existing script built/pushed the primary app image** — `infra/build_push_migrate_image.sh` has existed since #124; every prior build/push of `south-st-pete-app` (#120, #122) was manual, undocumented `docker build`/`docker push`. Added `infra/build_push_app_image.sh`, mirroring the migrate script's structure.

**`deploy-review` pre-commit: three rounds, real findings each time, all applied — including one this session caught in itself.**
- Round 1 (`Dockerfile.migrate` alone): HIGH (a draft comment cited this very entry, "#137," before it existed in the file — the entry now lands in the same commit set as the code, not after) + MEDIUM (a draft comment asserted a specific, unverifiable claim about how `south-st-pete-migrate` invokes transcription code — softened to state only the evidenced failure, not an unconfirmed mechanism) + LOW (wrong commit attribution for when the root Dockerfile gained ffmpeg — `6374cc2`, not #130's commit). All applied.
- Round 2 (full diff, incl. the new build script): HIGH — **the images this session had already built and pushed were tagged with the pre-fix commit SHA (`6bf13bc`) that doesn't actually contain the ffmpeg fix**, because the fix only entered git history once committed, and the build happened before that commit — the exact "tag doesn't match content" failure mode this whole entry exists to close, caught recurring inside the act of fixing it. Fixed by adding a dirty-tree guard (`git diff --quiet`/`git diff --cached --quiet`) to both build scripts, refusing to build against an unclean tree, then genuinely redoing the build/push/redeploy from a clean, committed tree. LOW: a garbled sentence in this entry's draft, copyedited.
- **Self-caught after round 2's own fix, before this entry was finalized:** the dirty-tree guard was written into both `infra/build_push_app_image.sh` and `infra/build_push_migrate_image.sh`, but only the migrate script's edit was actually re-staged before the resulting commit (`9b8968e`) — `build_push_app_image.sh` had already been `git add`ed *before* the guard was written to it, and was never re-added after, so the commit silently landed without the guard on that file despite the commit message claiming "both." Caught immediately after commit by re-checking `git show HEAD:infra/build_push_app_image.sh` rather than trusting the commit message — a second, small commit (`63192ab`) added the missing guard for real. No image was built or pushed with the gap live in between; caught before any consequence, not after one. Named here plainly rather than folded silently into "the fix," per this project's own standing rule that a mistake gets owned, not smoothed over.

**Real, live-verified redeploy — every claim re-checked against the actual running AWS resource, not the API call succeeding, using the final clean-tree images tagged `63192ab`:**
- Built and pushed `south-st-pete-app:63192ab` and `south-st-pete-app-migrate:63192ab` (the earlier, dirty-tree-tagged `6bf13bc` images were deleted from both ECR repos after the redo, rather than left as a live SHA/content mismatch for someone to trip over later).
- Registered `south-st-pete-worker:4` directly (**deliberately did not run `infra/register_app_taskdefs.sh`**, which registers `api` and `worker` together — `south-st-pete-api`'s task-def/service was out of scope for this fix, per explicit instruction).
- `update-service --task-definition south-st-pete-worker:4 --force-new-deployment`, polled until `rolloutState: COMPLETED` (`running:1`/`desired:1`), then separately `describe-tasks` on the live task ARN confirmed its real `taskDefinitionArn` is `:4`.
- One-off `run-task` against `south-st-pete-worker:4` (`containerOverrides`, not the persistent service) executed inside the real running image: `FFMPEG=/usr/bin/ffmpeg`, `FFPROBE=/usr/bin/ffprobe`, `SPLIT_TARGET_FRACTION=0.9`, `MAX_RESPLIT_ATTEMPTS=5`, `HAS_OLD_ERROR_TEXT=False`, exit code 0.
- Registered `south-st-pete-migrate:5` via `infra/register_migrate_taskdef.sh`, ran the equivalent check via `infra/run_migration_task.sh` (resolves the family to its latest ACTIVE revision) — identical real output, independently confirmed via `describe-tasks` that the task that ran was really `:5`.
- Deregistered superseded `south-st-pete-migrate:3` and `:4` (both INACTIVE now). Left `south-st-pete-worker`'s older `:2`/`:3` revisions registered, matching the always-on services' existing precedent (#122) of not deregistering superseded service revisions — note `:3` now points at a since-deleted ECR image (the dirty-tree-tagged `6bf13bc`), so it would fail to launch if anyone tried to reuse it; harmless since nothing does, but real, so recorded here rather than left implicit.

**The weekly recrawl schedule needed re-pointing twice this session — and DECISIONS #134's characterization of how it resolves the task-def ARN was checked against the actual script, not trusted, and found imprecise.** #134 described `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` as resolving the ARN "via a live `describe-task-definition` call at run time, not a guessed/hardcoded revision number" — true, but the "run time" is the *script's* run time, not EventBridge Scheduler's invocation time each Saturday. The resolved, revision-qualified ARN is baked into `Target.EcsParameters.TaskDefinitionArn` at `create-schedule`/`update-schedule` time and stays fixed until the script runs again — confirmed live both times: the schedule was found pointed at `:3` (stale) after `:4` was registered, and had to be explicitly re-run to move to `:4`; then again after `:5` superseded `:4`, re-run again to reach `:5`. Without this, the next scheduled Saturday recrawl would have run a stale, ffmpeg-missing image regardless of every other fix here.

**Full test suite not re-run this round** — no application code changed (only `infra/Dockerfile.migrate` and two `infra/build_push_*.sh` scripts), and this project's own convention (#130) is that real `ffmpeg`/`pydub` behavior is validated by the live AWS run, not the unit suite.

Why: This project's own standing distinction between "should work" and "REAL-verified" (#104/#125/#130) is exactly what this gap was — #130's own entry describes real, careful validation of the *code*, but that validation never propagated to what was actually running in AWS, and nothing caught it until this session's live audit. The two production failures looked similar (both surfaced as a transcription job failing) but had different root causes needing different fixes. The dirty-tree tagging bug this session caught in its own fix, and the guard-didn't-land mistake caught right after, are recorded with the same plainness as #120's/#131's/#135's prior self-disclosures — this project's honesty standard applies to today's session's own mistakes, not only to past ones.
Date: 2026-08-24

## #138 — `south-st-pete-worker`/`south-st-pete-migrate` were sized before #130's decode-to-PCM split path existed; both real-OOM-crashed on the actual largest known meeting; resized against a real measured peak, not a guess, with a real, disclosed monthly cost increase

Decision: Two consecutive real crashes on `south-st-pete-worker` while processing the two orphaned Granicus jobs left `status='claimed'` after DECISIONS #137's redeploy — confirmed live via `aws ecs describe-tasks` on both stopped task ARNs, not inferred: `exitCode: 137`, `containerReason: "OutOfMemoryError: container killed due to memory usage"`, one after 33m39s (03:21:22–03:55:01), one after 8m55s (03:55:35–04:04:30), both on `south-st-pete-worker:4` (`cpu=256, memory=512`). Root cause: that sizing predates #130 — `app/granicus/audio_split.py` decodes the full downloaded MP3 to raw PCM via `pydub`/`ffmpeg` before slicing, and 512 MiB was set when the original design (#86) aborted on any oversized file rather than decoding it.

**A citation error in this round's own brief, caught and corrected before it shaped the sizing math.** The brief's cited figure ("229,683,110 bytes / ~194.2 minutes / ~103 kbps") conflates two different real meetings. Per `app/granicus/worker.py`'s own module docstring, 229,683,110 bytes is the **Aug 13 2026** City Council session (the largest of DECISIONS #116's 3 real meetings); the 194.2 min / ~103 kbps figures belong to the separately-validated, smaller **Aug 6 2026** file (150,348,471 bytes, DECISIONS #130's real end-to-end validation run). Real measurement below (222.1 min, 137.9 kbps observed on the actual Aug 13 file) confirms the Aug-13 pairing was the correct one and settles which real number this round's sizing should anchor to.

**Worked math, then real measurement superseding it — same "estimate is not the guarantee" standard #130 held itself to.** A back-of-envelope PCM estimate (44.1kHz/16-bit/stereo decode assumption, since the real source sample format wasn't independently confirmed) at the real 229,683,110-byte file's ~222 min duration: 222 min × 60 × 176,400 bytes/sec ≈ 2.19 GiB for the full-file decode buffer alone, plus transient per-piece slice/re-export overhead (~10 pieces per #130's own split math, one active at a time, ~220–300 MiB), the `ffmpeg` subprocess's own RSS, and Python/psycopg/openai baseline — a plausible-but-unverified estimate landing around 3.8–4 GiB. **This estimate turned out to be a real underestimate, and was not used as the final sizing basis** — real measurement is:

Built `infra/_mem_probe.py` (new, committed one-off diagnostic, same convention as `infra/diag_granicus.py`) — downloads a real Granicus file via the existing `_download_ranged`, runs it through the actual `split_audio_for_whisper` path, and polls real cgroup memory (`/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.current`, falling back to the v1 path) on a background thread every 0.5s to capture the true peak **across the whole container** (parent Python process + any `ffmpeg` child), which is what Fargate's OOM killer actually enforces against — not just one process's own `getrusage` maxrss. Ran it as a one-off `aws ecs run-task` against a deliberately oversized test revision (`south-st-pete-migrate:6`, `cpu=2048/memory=8192`, chosen generously so the measurement wouldn't be truncated by hitting its own ceiling) against the real Aug 13 2026 file (`stpete_c1d2b310-...`, its `mp3_url` read via a read-only `SELECT` — the `granicus_transcription_jobs` rows themselves were never touched, per this round's explicit instruction).

**Real result:** downloaded 229,683,110 bytes in 56.0s; decoded+split into 10 pieces in 261.2s; real duration 13,328.0s (222.1 min), real observed bitrate 137,865 bps (137.9 kbps) — both independently confirming the corrected Aug-13 pairing above, and matching `worker.py`'s own ~222 min/137.7 kbps derivation almost exactly. **`PEAK_CONTAINER_MEMORY_BYTES = 7,393,656,832 (7,051.1 MiB / ≈6.88 GiB)`** — reached organically, well under the 8,192 MiB test ceiling (never hit/truncated by it), so this is a genuine workload-driven peak, not an artifact of the test's own headroom.

**Final sizing: `cpu=2048, memory=9216` for both `south-st-pete-worker` and `south-st-pete-migrate`**, sized consistently since both run the identical `audio_split.py` path. 9,216 MiB gives 2,165 MiB (≈30.7%) real margin over the measured 7,051 MiB peak — in the same ~30-35% range #130 itself used, not the ~13% margin `crawler-review` caught and corrected there. Fargate constrains memory to fixed values per cpu tier, checked live: `register-task-definition` itself validates and would reject an invalid pairing (an authoritative live check, stronger than a docs fetch, which this session also tried and got no usable table back from) — both the worker (`:5`) and migrate (`:7`) registrations succeeded against `cpu=2048/memory=9216`, a real, accepted Fargate pairing (2 vCPU tier's valid memory range is 4096–16384 MiB in 1024 MiB steps; 9216 is on that grid). `cpu=1024`'s own ceiling (max 8192 MiB) was ruled out outright — pushed to its own max it sits only ~16% *above* the measured 7,051 MiB peak (1,141 MiB headroom), thinner than this project's own established margin standard.

**Disclosed, not swept under the rug: this sizing is anchored to the real largest meeting seen to date (229 MB), not to `MAX_TOTAL_DOWNLOAD_BYTES` (500 MiB), the worker's own outer sanity ceiling.** Extrapolating the measured peak linearly by file size (peak scales with decoded duration, which scales with bytes at roughly constant bitrate) projects ≈15.7 GiB at the full 500 MiB ceiling — a `cpu=4096/memory≈20480` tier, dramatically more expensive, and one this round's brief scoped sizing to real observed evidence for, not that theoretical (per `worker.py`'s own words) "never-yet-observed... extreme boundary." A future real meeting materially longer than 229 MB (a long joint session, a multi-item marathon meeting) could still OOM at this sizing before hitting the 500 MiB abort ceiling — a real residual gap, named here rather than left implicit, for whoever revisits this if it happens.

**Real, live-verified redeploy — every claim checked against the actual running resource:**
- Registered `south-st-pete-worker:5` directly (not via `register_app_taskdefs.sh`, which also touches `api` — out of scope this round, same discipline as #137).
- `update-service --task-definition south-st-pete-worker:5 --force-new-deployment`, polled to `rolloutState: COMPLETED`, then independently `describe-tasks` on the live task ARN confirmed `taskDefinitionArn: .../south-st-pete-worker:5`, `cpu: "2048"`, `memory: "9216"`, `lastStatus: RUNNING`.
- Registered `south-st-pete-migrate:7` via `infra/register_migrate_taskdef.sh`. Did not re-run the standalone decode test against `:7` specifically — the measured 7,051 MiB peak was reached without hitting the *lower* 8,192 MiB test ceiling, so it's real evidence the workload fits under the *higher* 9,216 MiB production ceiling too, without needing a second real run to say so.
- Deregistered the superseded `south-st-pete-migrate:5` (old 512/1024 sizing) and the throwaway test-only `:6` (2048/8192). Left `south-st-pete-worker`'s older `:2`–`:4` registered, matching #122/#137's established precedent of not deregistering superseded always-on-service revisions.
- **Recrawl schedule needed re-pointing again**, same gap #137 already named (EventBridge Scheduler bakes in a revision-qualified ARN at schedule-update time, does not auto-follow the family's latest revision): re-ran `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh`, then independently confirmed via `aws scheduler get-schedule` that `Target.EcsParameters.TaskDefinitionArn` now reads `.../south-st-pete-migrate:7`.

**Real, disclosed monthly cost increase for the always-on `south-st-pete-worker` service** — confirmed via AWS's own Fargate pricing-page calculation constants ($0.000011244/vCPU-second, $0.000001235/GB-second → $0.0404784/vCPU-hr, $0.004446/GB-hr; a live `pricing:GetProducts` API check was attempted first and denied — this IAM user has no Pricing API access, not requested this round, so the pricing page's own worked example was used instead of a guess):
- Old (`256`/`512`, 730 hr/month): (0.25 × 0.0404784 + 0.5 × 0.004446) × 730 ≈ **$9.01/month**.
- New (`2048`/`9216`, 730 hr/month): (2 × 0.0404784 + 9 × 0.004446) × 730 ≈ **$88.31/month**.
- **Delta: ≈ +$79.30/month, an ≈9.8x increase**, reported in full rather than understated — this is the real, ongoing price of a decode-shaped workload on an always-on service, not a rounding-error line item. `south-st-pete-migrate` is not always-on (one-off runs + the weekly recrawl schedule), so its cost impact is per-invocation at the same higher rate, not a comparable standing monthly delta.

**Reconciled against the live $100/month budget alert (DECISIONS #133) — `deploy-review` pre-commit finding, MEDIUM: this wasn't checked before the first draft of this entry.** `south-st-pete-api` (confirmed live, unchanged, out of scope this round) is still `cpu=256/memory=512` ≈ $9.01/month. `south-st-pete-dev-db` is confirmed live as `db.t4g.micro`, single-AZ, 20 GiB storage. This IAM user has no Pricing API access (same denial as above), and a live pricing-page fetch for RDS returned no usable rate table this session, so the RDS figure below is a recalled, not independently live-confirmed, on-demand rate (~$0.016/vCPU-hr compute + ~$0.10-0.115/GB-month gp2/gp3 storage — flagged honestly as an estimate, not re-verified against a live source, unlike the Fargate figures above which came from AWS's own published calculation constants):
- `south-st-pete-worker` (new): ≈$88.31/mo
- `south-st-pete-api` (unchanged): ≈$9.01/mo
- `south-st-pete-dev-db` (estimate, not live-confirmed): ≈$11.68/mo compute + ≈$2.30/mo storage ≈ $14.00/mo
- **Combined always-on infra total: ≈$111.32/month — already over the $100/month budget alert threshold** (excludes OpenAI usage-based spend, tracked separately, and minor line items like ECR/CloudWatch Logs storage). This means the 100% `ACTUAL`/`GREATER_THAN` notification (#133) will very likely fire this cycle on infra cost alone, not as a false alarm — worth Amber knowing before it does, not after.

**Real-verify item deliberately left to Amber, per this round's explicit instruction:** the two orphaned `granicus_transcription_jobs` rows (`City Council`, 2026-07-23 and 2026-08-13) were read only (`SELECT`, for their `mp3_url`s), never reset — Amber resets them herself and watches the now-correctly-sized `south-st-pete-worker:5` process them for real.

**Update, same day: Amber did reset both rows, and both real jobs completed successfully at this sizing — closed out under #139 below, along with a follow-on cost-architecture change and a real, unrelated secret exposure found while diagnosing it.**

**`deploy-review` pre-commit pass: 3 MEDIUM + 3 LOW, all applied or addressed except one review-scope note.** No CRITICAL — nothing resembling a live credential in either taskdef template (unchanged placeholder tokens, only `cpu`/`memory` values edited) or `infra/_mem_probe.py` (confirmed to never touch `granicus_transcription_jobs` or any DB table). MEDIUM: this budget reconciliation section (above) was missing from the first draft — added. MEDIUM: `infra/_mem_probe.py`'s fixed `memprobe.mp3` filename could collide if the diagnostic is ever run twice concurrently in the same container — fixed to match `worker.py`'s own `granicus-{uuid4().hex}.mp3` uniqueness convention. MEDIUM: the background cgroup-memory poller only caught `FileNotFoundError`/`ValueError`, so any other exception would silently kill it mid-run and understate `PEAK_CONTAINER_MEMORY_BYTES` with no fail-loud signal on a number this decision's entire sizing rests on — fixed to catch and report any poller exception explicitly before printing the peak. LOW, applied: this entry's own "sits *below* the measured peak" wording had the direction backwards (8,192 MiB is *above* 7,051 MiB — that's why there's positive ~16% headroom; the math was always correct, only the sentence was wrong) — corrected. LOW, applied: `_mem_probe.py` had no cleanup of the downloaded `dest` file on a failure path before `split_audio_for_whisper` even runs — wrapped in `try/finally`, matching `.claude/rules/data.md`'s best-effort-cleanup convention `audio_split.py` itself already follows. LOW, review-scope note (no code/entry change): `deploy-review` had no live AWS credentials in its own environment, so it could not independently re-run `describe-tasks`/`get-schedule` to confirm the live redeploy claims itself — it confirmed the `cpu=2048/memory=9216` pairing is valid against the public Fargate grid on paper and found the diff/entry internally consistent, but flagged that gap explicitly rather than silently vouching for live state it couldn't check.

Why: The same "should work" vs. "REAL-verified" distinction #104/#125/#130/#137 already established applies here — a plausible back-of-envelope PCM estimate (≈3.8–4 GiB) undershot the real measured peak (7,051 MiB) by roughly half, which is exactly why this round built a real measurement tool against a real file instead of registering a task def off the estimate alone. Naming the citation error in the brief mattered because sizing math built on the wrong file's duration/bitrate would have been wrong in a way that looked internally consistent — catching it before the memory math was finalized, not after, follows this project's own "verify before acting on a given figure" discipline (#104/#122/#130). The cost delta is real and reported at its full size, not softened, because this project tracks Fargate spend explicitly (#133) and a 9.8x increase on an always-on service is a decision Amber is entitled to see in full, not a number rounded down to look smaller than it is.
