## #151 — Two real Trusted Advisor findings on `south-st-pete-dev-db` closed: automated backups enabled (7-day retention) and storage autoscaling enabled (50 GiB ceiling); `StorageEncrypted`/`MultiAZ` deliberately left untouched

Decision: Confirmed live via `aws rds describe-db-instances` (not assumed from the Trusted Advisor dashboard) before touching anything: `south-st-pete-dev-db` (`db.t4g.micro`, gp3, 20 GiB allocated, single-AZ, `StorageEncrypted: false`) had `BackupRetentionPeriod: 0` (zero automated backups) and `MaxAllocatedStorage: null` (storage autoscaling off) — real gaps on the instance holding this project's entire live knowledge base, not a false-positive advisory item.

**Retention period: 7 days.** AWS's own suggested default, and it lines up with a real operating-pattern fact already in this project's record: the Saturday recrawl schedule (#126/#134) is the dominant source of new writes, so 7-day retention means worst case a restore point exists from before the most recent weekly recrawl — proportionate to a solo-operator civic tool that (per CLAUDE.md's own framing) doesn't need aggressive point-in-time recovery, not the "why not 35" reflex a high-write production system would warrant.

**Storage ceiling: 50 GiB, worked from real evidence, not a round-number guess.** CloudWatch `FreeStorageSpace` over the full project lifetime (2026-08-19 through today) shows real usage of only ≈3.2 GB of the 20 GiB currently allocated (≈16% utilized), and recent daily deltas are near-flat (≈10.6 MB over the 2026-08-19→08-27 window) — expected, because the dominant future growth driver, the full 12-month Granicus backfill, has NOT run yet (blocked on #139/#150's still-open EventBridge Scheduler AWS Support case; only 4 real meetings are transcribed and embedded so far, per #116/#138/#139). Projected the full-backfill's real footprint from confirmed per-chunk math rather than guessing: `text-embedding-3-small` is confirmed 1536-dim (#67, live-verified), so each embedding is ≈6.1 KB raw plus a comparable amount again for the pgvector index, plus modest text/metadata/row overhead — call it ≈15 KB/chunk all-in. At a generously-estimated ≈150 chunks per multi-hour meeting transcript and a generously-estimated ≈50 meetings across a full year of St. Petersburg City Council sessions, that's ≈2.25 MB/meeting × 50 ≈ 113 MB for the entire remaining backfill — even padded 5x for the kind of underestimate #138 itself hit on a different (memory) estimate, that's still under 1 GB. Weekly Tier-1 recrawls (grants/BCC/PCF, text-only, idempotent-upsert per #58) add negligible net-new bytes on top. **50 GiB (2.5x the current 20 GiB base) was chosen as real safety margin for the unexpected** — WAL/index bloat, vacuum lag, a future meeting materially larger than any seen to date (#138 named this same residual-gap shape for worker memory sizing) — sized generously because, unlike Fargate `cpu`/`memory`, a higher `MaxAllocatedStorage` ceiling costs nothing extra unless actually consumed; the realistic full-maturity need computed above is well under it, so 50 GiB is headroom, not a number the project expects to approach soon.

**`--apply-immediately` used — and it caused a real, brief interruption my own prior expectation got wrong.** Before applying, checked live `DatabaseConnections` (CloudWatch, zero connections in the hour leading up to the change) specifically so this wouldn't land during real API/worker traffic. I expected (recalled, not verified beforehand) that enabling backup retention and storage autoscaling on RDS Postgres are both "dynamic" parameters applied without a restart. **That expectation was wrong for the backup-retention change** — `aws rds describe-events` shows a real `DB instance shutdown` at 03:26:59 UTC and `DB instance restarted` at 03:27:15 UTC (≈16 seconds), triggered by "Enabled automated backups," immediately followed by "Backing up DB instance." Naming this plainly rather than quietly: I proceeded on a recalled assumption that turned out to not hold for this specific change, and a real (if brief) restart happened as a result. The mitigating fact is also real, not assumed after the fact — `DatabaseConnections` `Maximum` stayed at `0.0` for every 1-minute datapoint from 03:15 to 03:30 UTC, spanning the entire restart window, so no live query was actually in flight to interrupt. `MaxAllocatedStorage` itself applied with no shutdown event at all (storage autoscaling is genuinely dynamic; only the backup-retention leg triggered the restart).

**Real-verify, post-change, live state (not just the API call's return value):**
- `BackupRetentionPeriod: 7` (was 0).
- `MaxAllocatedStorage: 50` (was `null`).
- `LatestRestorableTime: 2026-08-29T03:27:42Z` — confirms a real automated backup actually completed, not just that retention is configured.
- `PreferredBackupWindow` unchanged at `07:34-08:04` — this was already set despite retention being 0 before today; it's an inert default RDS assigns at instance creation regardless of whether backups are enabled, not a sign of a prior half-configured state. Nothing further needed there.
- `StorageEncrypted: false` and `MultiAZ: false` both independently re-confirmed unchanged in the same `describe-db-instances` call — explicitly out of scope this round (encryption needs a snapshot/restore-to-new-instance migration with real downtime; Multi-AZ roughly doubles RDS cost, a spend call like #138's, not a unilateral change) and neither was touched.

**No `infra/` provisioning script exists for this RDS instance** — checked (`grep` across `infra/` for `create-db-instance`/`BackupRetentionPeriod`/etc., one hit: `wait-and-run-smoke-test.sh`, which only polls status/reads the endpoint, never creates the instance). Consistent with DECISIONS #2's no-IaC-this-cycle scope — the instance was stood up manually. Real gap named, not fixed here: if this instance is ever recreated, these settings (and the Trusted Advisor gaps they closed) would silently regress unless someone remembers to reapply them by hand. Building a creation script would itself be a scope call (bumping against #2) — flagged for Amber, not unilaterally added.

Why: this is a real-verified fix to a real gap on the live production database holding this project's entire knowledge base (weeks of crawled content, real Granicus transcripts, real embedding spend) — not a dashboard checkbox exercise. The retention/ceiling numbers are both worked from live evidence (CloudWatch usage history, confirmed embedding dimensionality, this project's own documented backfill/recrawl cadence) in the same "estimate, then check it against something real" discipline #130/#138 already established, and the wrong-expectation-caused-a-restart moment is disclosed in full rather than smoothed over, matching this project's standing "REAL-verified, not assumed" bar.
Date: 2026-08-29

## #152 — DECISIONS #140's exposed credential rotated for real: new access key issued, old one deactivated then deleted, the exposing IAM tag removed

Decision: Amber directed the rotation `#140`/`#141` recommended. Confirmed first, not assumed: `AKIAXQWHJDS7OR4MF7JU` (the key ID found in `south-st-pete-infra-dev`'s IAM tag) was not a stray or unrelated credential — it was the real, active access key this entire session's `AWS_PROFILE=south-st-pete` work had been authenticating with the whole time, created `2026-08-20`. Rotated in place without any session interruption: issued a new access key (`aws iam create-access-key`, output redirected straight to a local file, never printed to this transcript, per `.claude/rules/secrets.md`), rewrote `~/.aws/credentials`'s `[south-st-pete]` section from that file via a small Python script that never echoed either the new key ID or secret, verified the new credentials work (`sts get-caller-identity` — safe to print, key *IDs* aren't secret, only the secret-access-key half is), deactivated the old key, re-verified the session still worked post-deactivation, then deleted the old key outright (no reason to keep a confirmed-exposed key around even inactive) and removed the exposing tag (`untag-user`, key `AKIAXQWHJDS7OR4MF7JU`) from the IAM user. Final state, real-verified: exactly one access key on the user, `AKIAXQWHJDS7D5G7L6PP`, active; zero tags. The temp file holding the new key material was deleted immediately after use.

Why: closes `#140`/`#141`'s real, disclosed exposure for real — the credential is rotated, the old one is gone (not just deactivated), and the tag that exposed it in the first place no longer exists, rather than the finding sitting as a documented-but-unactioned risk.

## #153 — `south-st-pete-migrate` was running #146's code from before it existed; redeployed and real-verified, first genuine `run_live_recrawl.py run` against production now recorded in `crawl_runs`

Decision: Amber asked directly to crawl all Tier-1 sources for real. `crawl_runs` had zero rows despite `chunks` already holding content (from the original frozen-fixture build, not a live crawl) — this was the first real attempt to run `scripts/run_live_recrawl.py run` against production. It failed immediately with a real traceback: `run()` calling `build_all_chunks_live()`, the old monolithic pre-`#146` function, not `_build_source_segments()` (`#146`'s per-source-isolated structure, committed `42b457d`, already code-reviewed and landed). Real gap found: `south-st-pete-migrate`'s deployed ECR image was last built during `#138`'s work (OOM-sizing fix), predating `#146` entirely. The triggering error inside that stale run was a real `pinellascf.org` `robots.txt` connection timeout — but because the deployed code lacked `#146`'s isolation, one source's timeout crashed the entire run instead of being caught and recorded, with the other 12 sources never attempted.

**Redeployed from current HEAD (`03650a1`, clean tree).** One correction to how this was directed: built via `infra/build_push_migrate_image.sh`, not `infra/build_push_app_image.sh` — the latter builds and pushes `south-st-pete-app` (the deployed api/worker image, a separate ECR repo), while `infra/register_migrate_taskdef.sh` resolves images from `south-st-pete-app-migrate` specifically (DECISIONS #120's deliberate repo split, so migration tooling and what's actually running in ECS never get confused). Using the app-image script would have pushed to the wrong repo and left `register_migrate_taskdef.sh` unable to find the tag. Pushed `516887747774.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/south-st-pete-app-migrate:03650a1`, registered `south-st-pete-migrate:11` against it.

**Real-verified the new revision actually runs `#146`'s code before trusting it with a real recrawl** — not inferred from "a new revision was registered." A one-off task ran `hasattr(scripts.run_live_recrawl, '_build_source_segments')` (`True`) and `hasattr(scripts.run_live_recrawl, 'build_all_chunks_live')` (`False`, confirming the old monolith is gone) against the live deployed image.

**Real recrawl attempt, run_id `bece67c4-3cfd-4f56-a01a-a0efa18fa1fb`: all 13 source segments succeeded**, including `pinellas_cf` — the earlier `robots.txt` timeout was transient, not a real site outage or a parser regression (0 inserted / 7 skipped, i.e. all 7 chunks already present and unchanged, same idempotent-upsert shape as every other source). Totals across all sources: `inserted=77, skipped=279, chunks_failed=0`. Real-verified independently of the script's own stdout: queried `crawl_runs` directly via a second one-off task using `app.db.connection.get_connection()` (not hand-rolled psycopg2 — the deployed image only carries `psycopg` v3, and the container's real env vars are `LOCAL_DB_*`, not `RDS_*`, per `infra/migrate-taskdef.template.json`) — 13 rows, all `outcome='success'`, matching the run's own totals exactly.

One process note, not a secrets exposure: an initial verification attempt (multi-line `python -c "..."`) silently no-op'd — exit 0, empty logs — because `run_migration_task.sh`'s command-array builder (`printf '%s\n' "$@" | python -c 'json.dumps([...for l in sys.stdin])'`) splits on embedded newlines inside a single argument, so a multi-line `-c` string gets fragmented into multiple array elements and only the first line actually reaches `python -c`. Worked around by keeping override commands single-line; not fixed in the script itself since this run's scope was the redeploy and recrawl, not `run_migration_task.sh`'s command-encoding.

Left `south-st-pete-migrate:7` through `:10` (the stale, pre-`#146` revisions plus older ones before that) registered/ACTIVE rather than deregistering — out of scope for this task, flagged here rather than silently left implicit. `#129` already accepts `south-st-pete-migrate` staying permanently ACTIVE (not deregistered after one-off use) so the still-blocked EventBridge Scheduler (`#150`, AWS Support case 178797281900472, untouched) has something to invoke once unblocked; `#137`/`#138` separately established a practice of deregistering *superseded* migrate revisions specifically, which lapsed somewhere after `#138` (four old revisions accumulated, not just one). Not cleaned up here since it wasn't part of the assigned task and doesn't block anything real — worth a deliberate pass later.

Why: this is the first real, live-verified confirmation that `#146`'s per-source failure isolation works against production, not just against local docker-compose and a synthetic induced 404 (`#146`'s own pre-commit verification). It also closes a real deploy-staleness gap that would have silently undermined every future recrawl (weekly schedule included, once `#150` unblocks) by running stale code indefinitely with no signal that a new commit hadn't actually shipped. `crawl_runs` now holds the first real evidence, per source, of what this system's live Tier-1 crawl actually does against the real internet.
Date: 2026-08-29

## #154 — content_hash closes the disclosed content-staleness gap (real 593-row production backfill, both branches live-verified); real root cause + real fix for the "St. Petersburg's AMI" retrieval miss

Decision: Two `rag-pipeline` items this round, bundled per explicit direction, both landed at commit `32fa151`.

**Issue 1 — content-staleness.** `make_chunk_id` (app/chunking/base.py) is deliberately identity-derived, never text-derived — documented, correct behavior for its original purpose (idempotent re-runs), but it meant `embed_and_insert_chunk`'s old pre-check ("chunk_id already has a row -> skip") could never tell "genuinely unchanged" apart from "same identity, live text edited" — a gap `scripts/run_live_recrawl.py`'s own module docstring already disclosed but left open. Fix: a new `chunks.content_hash` column (sha256 hex digest of `chunk_text`, `app/db/schema.py`) with a nullable-then-locked-NOT-NULL migration path — a bare `ADD COLUMN ... NOT NULL` was never viable against the real, already-populated production table. `embed_and_insert_chunk`'s pre-check is now: no row -> insert; row exists + hash matches -> skip (free, no API call — the property that makes the weekly recrawl, DECISIONS #126/#134, cheap); row exists + hash differs (including a still-NULL stored hash, the real transient pre-backfill state) -> re-embed (real, paid call) and UPSERT in place via the existing `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (same chunk_id, never a duplicate row). `PipelineResult` gained a fourth outcome, `updated`, distinct from `inserted`/`skipped`/`failed`. New `scripts/backfill_content_hash.py` backfills existing rows then re-applies the schema migration to lock NOT NULL for real.

**Real backfill evidence, live RDS, not local:** ran via the established one-off-ECS-task pattern (DECISIONS #124/#143 — `infra/run_migration_task.sh` against the registered `south-st-pete-migrate` task family, RDS not being publicly reachable, DECISIONS #120). Rebuilt+pushed `south-st-pete-app-migrate:32fa151`, registered `south-st-pete-migrate:12`. `scripts/backfill_content_hash.py` against the real live corpus: **593 row(s) found with content_hash IS NULL — backfilled 593, remaining NULL rows: 0, content_hash column is_nullable: NO.** (593, not the ~356 estimate given going in — the corpus has grown since that count was taken; real evidence superseded the stale estimate rather than being forced to match it.) Also run and verified locally first, against the real local dev DB's own 521 pre-existing rows, before ever touching RDS — same clean result (0 remaining NULLs, locked NOT NULL).

**Both branches proven with real production data, not synthetic fixtures** — using Issue 2's own real fix as the natural real-world trigger (chunk_id unchanged, text genuinely changed): running just the `ami_table` segment (`StpeteIncomeLimitsCrawler` + `chunk_ami_thresholds` + `embed_and_insert_chunks`, live fetch + live OpenAI + live RDS) against the same 7 real chunk_ids twice —
- **First run (update branch):** `inserted=0 updated=7 skipped=0 failed=0`.
- **Second, immediate re-run (skip branch):** `inserted=0 updated=0 skipped=7 failed=0` — zero API calls for the now-unchanged content.
- **Skip branch also confirmed on a source untouched by any code change this round** (`arpa`, 10 real chunks, live fetch): `inserted=0 updated=0 skipped=10 failed=0` — proves the mass backfill correctly preserved hashes for genuinely-unchanged content across the corpus, not just the two AMI-specific runs.

**Issue 2 — real, reproduced retrieval miss.** `POST /query {"question": "What is St. Petersburgs AMI"}` returned `not_in_corpus=True` despite 7 real `ami_threshold_table` chunks existing, all real, all already embedded. Root-caused live against RDS (real embedding, real pgvector cosine search, `CANDIDATE_POOL_SIZE=40`, `RELEVANCE_THRESHOLD=0.5`, `app/rag/retrieval.py` — unchanged by this round): **none of the 7 chunks reached the top-40 candidate pool for the exact failing phrasing.** The best-scoring one (`a04bb16b...`, the bare 100%-AMI benchmark) ranked **#52 globally at similarity 0.3582** — below even DECISIONS #73's own previously-established off-topic negative-control ceiling (0.42-0.46). The apostrophe variant ("St. Petersburg's AMI") got exactly 1 of 7 chunks into the pool, at rank #25, similarity 0.3952 — still well under threshold. Pool size was never the limiting factor (even a much larger `CANDIDATE_POOL_SIZE` wouldn't have helped — the real scores themselves were the problem, not where the cutoff sat).

**Real root cause:** the AMI chunk text (`app/chunking/ami_table.py`) never named a place or a source — just `"Area Median Income (AMI) eligibility thresholds for <program>:"` plus bare numeric rows — so a query naming a place had nothing to anchor to. Confirmed empirically, not by inspection alone: a real, live-embedded candidate revision of the chunk text (one added sentence) moved the SAME chunk's similarity to the two failing queries from 0.3683/0.4031 to **0.5584/0.6015** — and to the already-passing "Pinellas County AMI" query from 0.5722 to 0.6825 (improved, not regressed).

**Fix, deliberately scoped to be true, not just effective:** one sentence prepended to every AMI chunk — *"This table appears on the City of St. Petersburg's official website (stpete.org), on its housing income-limits reference page."* Chosen specifically because it is verifiable against this chunker's own one hard-coded source URL (income_limits.php) and makes NO claim about which government jurisdiction the dollar figures apply to. Checked live before writing it: the actual source page's raw HTML never contains the word "Pinellas" anywhere — confirmed by fetching the real page through the project's own `BaseCrawler` — so a sentence naming a county would have been fabricating provenance the source doesn't carry, exactly the failure mode `.claude/rules/rag.md` exists to prevent.

**rag-review pre-commit pass: 2 HIGH + 1 MEDIUM + 2 LOW, all applied.** HIGH — the shared framing sentence risked pushing the 7 AMI chunks' pairwise similarity toward the 0.97 dedup threshold (DECISIONS #73's own named "template-near-duplicate" failure shape); real-checked via live OpenAI embeddings of all 7 re-embedded chunks: max pairwise similarity **0.9478** (SHIP vs. HOME), safely under 0.97 — no collision. HIGH — `scripts/backfill_content_hash.py`'s per-row `UPDATE` could race a concurrent recrawl writer and clobber a fresh hash with a stale one; fixed with a conditional guard (`AND content_hash IS NULL`) plus `rowcount`-based reporting of any row a concurrent writer already won. MEDIUM — folding the new `updated` outcome into `crawl_runs.inserted` (that table has no `updated` column) silently changes what that column has meant since DECISIONS #146; documented explicitly in `app/db/crawl_runs.py`'s own docstring as `inserted+updated` going forward, and a real `updated` column is flagged as a disclosed, not-built-this-round follow-up for `api-layer`/the orchestrator (it touches `crawler_health.py`'s owned response shape, not just this table). Two LOW: `chunk_already_embedded()`'s docstring corrected to note it has no production caller anymore; `information_schema` lookups schema-qualified. 513/513 full suite (up from 503).

**Real end-to-end re-verification, against the live RDS corpus AND the actual public `south-st-pete-api` endpoint, not assumed:**
- The exact originally-failing query, through the real public endpoint (`http://<task-ip>:8000/query`): `"What is St. Petersburgs AMI"` -> `not_in_corpus:false`, a real grounded answer citing all 6 above-threshold AMI chunks by chunk_id (rule 4's multi-item disambiguation firing correctly).
- The apostrophe variant, and the previously-working `"What is the Pinellas County Area Median Income (AMI)?"` — both still correctly grounded (the latter: **no regression**, similarity improved).
- **scripts/validate_generation.py's full 5-query regression suite**, re-run live through the real `answer_query()` pipeline: Pinellas HCD ambiguity (DECISIONS #73), SHIP household-of-4, deep-energy-retrofits, SPHA rent-due-date — all still correctly grounded with the expected/plausible chunks. The "genuine not-in-corpus" query now returns a real, grounded answer (real Granicus transcript content exists in the corpus today that didn't when that script was written at 342 chunks) — a real change in corpus state over time, not a regression from this round's diff (retrieval.py/generation.py untouched).
- **DECISIONS #143 Question A** ("Which councilmember requested a referral about the private lateral rebate program...") — still correctly cites `70fb122bedac082dae2fe2a9f1238dbe`, the exact real fix target, not either of #143's two disclosed wrong chunks.
- **DECISIONS #139's Aug 13 invocation regression** (exact original wording not recorded verbatim, same disclosed gap #143 itself noted for the Aug 6 query — a reasonable equivalent phrasing used) — still correctly cites `2c1f990c49da3d7c5bef305f14f4625f`, Pastor Michael Hawkins.
- DECISIONS #130's Aug 6 Sky Isle/Veridian regression not re-run: unrelated to any file this round touches (no retrieval.py/generation.py/Granicus changes), and #143 already re-confirmed its non-match was a real property of that rephrasing, not a bug — re-running it would burn a real API call to reconfirm something this diff cannot have affected.

**Honest, disclosed residual finding, not chased further this round (matches the dispatch's own scoping):** the secondary jurisdiction-labeling gap flagged going in — a generated answer asserting a specific jurisdiction ("for Pinellas County") that the cited chunk's own text never actually states — is **not fixed by this round's change, and this round's own fix mechanism makes the same shape of overreach newly visible under "St. Petersburg" too.** The live re-verification above shows the model's answer to the fixed query opening with *"The Area Median Income (AMI) in St. Petersburg varies..."* — the chunk's new sentence only ever claims the table is *published on* stpete.org, never that the dollar figures apply specifically to St. Petersburg's jurisdiction (HUD AMI figures are typically defined at MSA/county level), but the model's prose compresses that into an implied jurisdictional claim regardless. This is the same class of gap as the pre-existing "for Pinellas County" phrasing (unaffected, still present, confirmed unchanged in the live re-verification above) — not introduced by this fix, but not closed by it either, and now present under two place-names instead of one. Flagged here plainly rather than left implicit, per this project's own disclosure convention; a real fix would need `_citation_is_supported()`/prompt-level work in `app/rag/generation.py` to stop the model asserting jurisdiction a cited chunk doesn't state, which is out of this round's scope.

**Production redeploy required beyond the migrate image, discovered and closed this round, not left as a silent gap:** `chunks.content_hash NOT NULL` (once locked) means the OLD, pre-`32fa151` `app/embeddings/pipeline.py` — which the live `south-st-pete-api`/`south-st-pete-worker` services were still running — would start hard-failing every new/changed chunk insert (`app/api/documents.py`'s `POST /documents/upload` calls `embed_and_insert_chunks` directly). Closed by rebuilding+pushing `south-st-pete-app:32fa151`, registering `south-st-pete-api:4`/`south-st-pete-worker:8`, force-redeploying both, and waiting for `services-stable` — real-verified live: `GET /health` -> `{"status":"ok","db":"ok"}`; a real end-to-end upload (`POST /documents/upload` with a fresh test file, real `X-Upload-Api-Key`) completed and became queryable with a correct citation, proving the api service's own `embed_and_insert_chunks` call now succeeds against the migrated schema; the test upload was then removed from the live corpus the same way DECISIONS #149 did (`DELETE FROM chunks`/`document_uploads` scoped to the exact known chunk_id/file_hash, `RETURNING` confirmed, re-queried to confirm `not_in_corpus:true` afterward). Separately found and closed: the weekly recrawl schedule (`south-st-pete-weekly-recrawl`) was still pointed at `south-st-pete-migrate:7` — real, live-confirmed via `get-schedule` before any fix — re-ran `infra/register_recrawl_schedule.sh` (idempotent update-in-place, no design change) to point it at `:12`; confirmed live via `get-schedule` afterward. `south-st-pete-migrate:12` left ACTIVE/registered, not deregistered, per DECISIONS #129's already-authorized tradeoff (the schedule needs an invokable revision). This redeploy work is mechanically deploy-infra's usual territory (established, previously-reviewed scripts, no new design decisions) — done here directly rather than left broken, since a content-staleness fix that ships a schema change but leaves production unable to write new content would be a regression, not a fix; flagged to the orchestrator/deploy-infra for awareness, not silently absorbed as if it were always rag-pipeline's job.

Why: both issues were named plainly rather than guessed at — `scripts/run_live_recrawl.py`'s own docstring already disclosed the content-staleness gap rather than hiding it, and Issue 2 was root-caused with real similarity scores against live RDS before any fix was written, matching this project's own standing discipline (DECISIONS #104/#125/#130/#137/#138/#142/#143/#153) that "should work" is never the same claim as "REAL-verified." Both branches of the content_hash logic, the real production backfill, the real chunk-text fix, and the real end-to-end query path were each proven against live production data and the live public endpoint, not assumed correct from code review or local tests alone — the same standard this project has held itself to since #104, applied here to a schema change and a retrieval-quality fix landing together for the first time.

Commit: `32fa151` (all code + tests). Pre-commit review: `rag-review`, 2 HIGH + 1 MEDIUM + 2 LOW, all applied before commit (see above). 513/513 full suite.
Date: 2026-08-29

## #155 — #139's scheduled-worker cutover re-investigated and NOT completed: the staged group-ARN "fix" is rejected (falsified by real evidence, not just doubted), a real live security walk-back it introduced is reverted, and the underlying `ValidationException` blocker from #139/#150 is confirmed to still be real and unexplained

Decision: Found two files (`infra/granicus-scheduler-trust-policy.template.json`, `infra/register_granicus_worker_schedule.sh`) staged-uncommitted from a prior session, changing the EventBridge Scheduler execution role's trust-policy `aws:SourceArn` condition from the specific schedule ARN to the schedule-GROUP ARN, with a comment attributing this to AWS Support case 178797281900472 having root-caused #139's blocker to ARN scoping. Live AWS state showed the IAM role (`south-st-pete-granicus-scheduler-execution-role`, created 2026-08-24) already had this group-ARN policy applied in production, but `aws scheduler create-schedule` had never actually been run against it — no schedule existed. The staged code was never committed.

**`deploy-review` flagged this before anything was applied, and its core objection held up against re-verification, not just suspicion:** #139 itself already tested a trust policy with *no* `ArnEquals` condition at all (the most permissive shape possible) and got the identical `ValidationException` — that result already falsifies "the fix is scoping the condition correctly," group ARN included, since the most-permissive case failed the same way a correctly-scoped case should not have. No actual AWS Support case text is quoted anywhere in the diff or this repo's history (unlike #150, which quoted its case verbatim) — the attribution to case 178797281900472 is unverified; this session has no AWS Support API access (`SubscriptionRequiredException`, same gap #150 hit) and no browser-driving capability to check the console directly, so the real case response could not be independently pulled this round.

**Three real, live, bounded tests run this session — not assumed — fully resolve the question of whether ARN-condition shape is the variable:**
1. No condition at all (#139's own prior test) → `ValidationException`, identical message.
2. Specific-schedule-ARN (the original, narrow, already-working-for-recrawl pattern), against the now 5+-week-old role → `ValidationException`, identical message. This also rules out #139's other standing hypothesis (new-IAM-role propagation delay) — 5 weeks is nowhere near a propagation window.
3. Schedule-GROUP-ARN (the staged diff's proposed fix), applied live as a deliberately bounded, immediately-reverted diagnostic → `ValidationException`, identical message.

All three fail identically. The ARN condition's shape was never the variable in #139's blocker — the staged diff's causal story is wrong, not merely unproven.

**Real security finding, independent of the causal question: group-ARN scoping is a real walk-back, not a neutral change.** It lets *any* schedule created in the `default` group (confirmed: the unrelated `south-st-pete-weekly-recrawl` already lives there too) assume this role — reversing the narrow-scoping-per-task-family philosophy #139 established in the same entry for this exact role. The live role was found already carrying this broader grant (applied by an unknown prior session, never committed, never documented) and has been reverted back to specific-schedule-ARN scoping for real (`aws iam update-assume-role-policy`), confirmed via `aws iam get-role`.

**Net result, real-verified:** staged diff discarded (`git restore --staged --worktree` on both files — repo now matches HEAD, nothing to commit on the code side). Live IAM role back to narrow scoping. No `south-st-pete-granicus-worker-schedule` exists (confirmed, `ResourceNotFoundException`) — no partial state left behind. `south-st-pete-worker` untouched (`desired=1`/`running=1`, confirmed) — per this round's explicit instruction, it stays as the safety net until a working scheduled (or equivalent on-demand) path is actually proven live, which did not happen this round. **The #139/#150 cost-cutover goal is not accomplished — the always-on worker is still accruing #138's disclosed ~$88/mo.**

**Flagged, not actioned, pending Amber's call:** the real requirement underneath #139's cost fix was never "must use EventBridge Scheduler" — it was "stop paying for 24/7 idle capacity" against a pipeline #139's own research found has no real-time requirement and whose meeting registration is already 100% manual. A manual on-demand invocation (`aws ecs run-task` against the existing, already-correctly-configured `south-st-pete-granicus-worker` task definition, run by Amber alongside her existing manual meeting-registration step — same permission model `infra/run_migration_task.sh` already uses, no new IAM trust relationship, no EventBridge Scheduler dependency at all) would sidestep this blocker entirely instead of continuing to spend diagnostic time on a `ValidationException` that has now survived three independent condition shapes. Not implemented this round — it's a real change to Amber's day-to-day workflow, not a pure infra fix, so it needs her sign-off before a specialist builds it.

Why: applying an unverified fix to a live security-sensitive trust policy — especially one `deploy-review` flagged and this session's own re-testing then disproved outright — would have papered over a real, still-unexplained AWS-side blocker with a cosmetic change plus a quiet security regression. Running the three bounded diagnostic tests live, rather than accepting or rejecting the theory on reasoning alone, is this project's own "REAL-verified, not assumed" standard (#152/#153) applied to a negative result, not just positive ones — a disproven hypothesis recorded with real evidence is worth more to whoever picks this up next than a vague "didn't seem right." Leaving `south-st-pete-worker` running rather than forcing a close on this entry protects the one thing that was explicitly not supposed to break while fixing this.
Date: 2026-10-04

## #156 — Amber's explicit sign-off: abandon the EventBridge Scheduler path entirely, replace it with a manually-run `aws ecs run-task` script

Decision: #155 left the real `ValidationException` blocker unresolved and flagged, as an option pending Amber's call, a manual on-demand trigger (`aws ecs run-task` under the operator's own AWS credentials, no EventBridge Scheduler, no new IAM trust relationship) as a way to sidestep rather than keep diagnosing it — since #139's own research already established this pipeline has no real-time requirement and meeting registration is already a 100%-manual step Amber performs herself. Amber reviewed that tradeoff directly (automated-but-blocked EventBridge path vs. manual-but-working `run-task` path) in conversation on 2026-10-04 and gave explicit sign-off to build and run the manual-trigger script instead of continuing to chase the scheduler blocker. This is the decision #155 itself deliberately did not make — #155's own text says "flagged, not actioned, pending Amber's call" — and no entry existed recording that the call had actually been made until this one; `infra/run_granicus_worker_task.sh`'s header cites this entry, not #155, as the source of that authorization.

**Verification scope (smoke test, 2026-10-05) — launch mechanism only, NOT transcription.** Amber could not supply a resolved direct mp3 URL for a real pending meeting (the human-resolution step #79 requires; no granicus.com account on her side for the test meeting), so the full end-to-end test is pending a real meeting. The smoke test instead ran against the live, unmodified queue state (`/health/crawlers`, live API): 4 total, 0 pending, 0 claimed, 4 completed. No row was registered, claimed, or touched by this run. Real evidence, all from `south-st-pete` profile, us-east-1:
- `infra/run_granicus_worker_task.sh` launched task `44d3042a0cba4d7d8ee84ca17817bb6d` (family `south-st-pete-granicus-worker:1`), went PROVISIONING -> PENDING -> RUNNING -> DEPROVISIONING -> STOPPED, `stopCode=EssentialContainerExited`, container exit code 0, script exit 0.
- CloudWatch stream `granicus-worker/south-st-pete-granicus-worker/44d3042a0cba4d7d8ee84ca17817bb6d` (log group `/ecs/south-st-pete-app`): `no stale claimed jobs to recover` then `queue empty, nothing to process this invocation`. Clean run, no tracebacks.
- Double-launch guard: while the first task was RUNNING, a second invocation of the script printed the refusal message and exited 1 without calling `run-task`. Verified live, not assumed.
- IAM: every call the script makes (`ecs:ListTasks`, `ecs:DescribeTaskDefinition`, `ecs:RunTask`, `ecs:DescribeTasks`, `logs:GetLogEvents`) succeeded under the existing operator profile — no new permissions were needed or granted.
- Queue after run: unchanged, 0 pending / 0 claimed / 4 completed. `south-st-pete-worker` untouched: `desiredCount=1`, `runningCount=1`.

**What this does NOT prove:** that a real meeting's audio is transcribed through this path, that a job's status column transitions correctly through a real `claimed` -> `completed`/`failed` cycle, or that the stored-failure-reason path (crawler.md fail-loud) fires for a real failure. Those remain unverified until a real pending meeting exists. The cost cutover is therefore **not** authorized by this entry: `south-st-pete-worker` stays at `desiredCount=1` until a real transcription is verified end-to-end and recorded in a follow-up entry.

Scope of what was authorized: building and manually running `infra/run_granicus_worker_task.sh` against the existing, already-registered `south-st-pete-granicus-worker` task definition (same image/cpu/memory as the always-on worker, DECISIONS #138's sizing) — no new IAM role, no EventBridge Scheduler resource, no change to `south-st-pete-api`. `south-st-pete-worker`'s scale-down to `desiredCount=0` is explicitly conditioned on the new manual path being proven live first (per this round's own instruction, mirroring #139's original "don't break what's not broken while fixing it" caution) — not authorized by this entry on its own; see the follow-up entry once that verification is real and complete.

Why: this project's DECISIONS.md discipline requires every design decision get a numbered entry at the time it's made, not batched or left implicit — #155 explicitly deferred this call to Amber rather than guessing, and once she made it, the record needed its own entry rather than letting a later entry (or a script's header comment) silently assume authorization that was never actually logged.
Date: 2026-10-04

## #157 — Amber's explicit sign-off: scale `south-st-pete-worker` to zero; Granicus transcription now runs only via the manual trigger script

Decision: Amber approved, in conversation on 2026-10-05, scaling the always-on `south-st-pete-worker` service to `desiredCount=0` and processing pending Granicus meetings through `infra/run_granicus_worker_task.sh` instead. Her words: "yes", in reply to the proposal to scale the service to zero, which would stop the 24/7 cost (roughly $88/month for the 2 vCPU / 9 GB task alone, estimated from published Fargate rates, not billing data).

Precondition: the Granicus queue was read as 0 pending / 0 claimed / 4 completed a few minutes before the change (orchestrator's live read). The agent re-read of the health endpoint could not be completed in this session, so that read is minutes old, not taken immediately before the change.

Executed by Amber in her own terminal (`$env:AWS_PROFILE="south-st-pete"; aws ecs update-service --cluster south-st-pete-dev --service south-st-pete-worker --desired-count 0`). Verified afterward: `desiredCount=0`, `runningCount=0`, `pendingCount=0`.

Rollback: `aws ecs update-service --cluster south-st-pete-dev --service south-st-pete-worker --desired-count 1`.

Not verified: transcription through the manual-trigger path. The first real pending meeting processed this way is the verification. Until then, the always-on worker is off, so a meeting registered now waits in the queue until the manual script is run.

Why: the always-on worker was the dominant known Fargate cost, and the scheduler path stayed blocked. Amber accepted the tradeoff of manual triggering and the unverified transcription path in exchange for stopping the cost.
Date: 2026-10-05

## #158 — Repurpose this repo in place into the Coalition Grant App; Phase A strips the civic-records tool to its reusable core

Decision: Amber directed, in conversation on 2026-10-19, that this repo become the multi-organization grant application tool described in the "Coalition Grant App — Requirements Questionnaire" (Oct. 6, 2026, Erica Hall's completed draft, FLFPC as administrator). She chose to repurpose **in place** over a fork, knowing that retires the South St. Petersburg civic-records tool. She also chose AWS Cognito for auth and MFA, and set the scope for now to Phase A plus a locally-kept design plan for B–F, with no B–F code until she signs off.

Supersedes: #11 (closed source list) and #12 (Granicus 12-month bound), since neither source exists anymore. The 9-day schedule (#9) and PLAN.md's interview-defense framing are retired, and PLAN.md is removed (still in git history). Still binding: #1 (Fargate), #2 (no K8s/IaC), #8 (one image), #13 (OpenAI), #131/#135 (secrets rules), #136 (built for a successor).

Phase A, this entry (code only):
- Dropped: `app/crawlers/`, `app/granicus/`, the 8 source-specific chunkers, `app/db/crawl_runs.py`, `GET /health/crawlers` plus its UI panel and schemas, all of `scripts/`, the crawler/granicus tests and fixtures, every worker/scheduler/migrate/diagnostic file in `infra/`, `infra/DEPLOY_NEXT_STEPS.md` (stale Phase K runbook), `DEMO.md`, `PLAN.md`, the `worker` compose service, ffmpeg in the image, `pydub`/`requests`/`beautifulsoup4`/`lxml`/`responses` deps, the `crawler`/`crawler-review` agents, and `.claude/rules/crawler.md`.
- Kept: `app/db`, `app/embeddings`, `app/rag`, `app/chunking/base.py` + `uploaded_document.py`, `POST /documents/upload`, `/query`, `/health`, `/sources/{doc_id}`, the static UI, Docker/compose, the `api` deploy scripts, and the cost alerts.
- Moved: `Attribution` from `app/crawlers/base.py` to `app/provenance.py`. The fail-loud and source-attribution rules moved from `crawler.md` into `.claude/rules/data.md`, since they apply to uploads and imports just as much.
- Fixed in passing: `infra/register_app_taskdefs.sh` looped over `api worker`; with the worker template gone it would have failed on the second pass. It now registers `api` only.
- Verified: `pytest` passes 121 tests, 0 skipped, against the real local pgvector Postgres.

Planned phases (detail in the local design plan, which gets its own adversarial review before any B code):
- B: Cognito auth + MFA, organizations, users, roles, time-limited per-application access.
- C: Per-organization document vault, with retrieval isolated per org.
- D: Opportunities, master-spreadsheet import/export, applications (LOI ↔ full), field limits.
- E: Cited field-by-field drafting, review/approval chain, comments, versions, audit log.
- F: Readiness checks, Word/package export, reminders.

Known residuals, deliberately not done in Phase A:
- `app/rag/generation.py`'s system prompt and grounding stopword lists are still worded for St. Petersburg civic records. Retargeting them changes generation behavior, so it belongs in Phase C/E with its own `rag-review` pass, not in a strip-down.
- The `chunks` table still has transcript-era nullable columns (`start_seconds`, `end_seconds`). Dropping columns is a live-schema change, so it's deferred to Phase C's schema work.
- Historical comments in kept code still cite deleted files (`scripts/…`, `PLAN.md Phase X`). They resolve in git history and are left alone rather than churning working code.

AWS state, deliberately NOT touched (each needs Amber's explicit sign-off, per #156/#157 precedent):
- The deployed `south-st-pete-api` still runs the pre-#158 image until rebuilt and redeployed.
- RDS still holds the civic-records corpus in `chunks`, plus the orphaned `crawl_runs` and `granicus_transcription_jobs` tables.
- `south-st-pete-worker` (desiredCount 0 per #157), the granicus-worker task family, and the weekly recrawl schedule from #126/#134 may still exist. Their live state was not re-read this session. If the recrawl schedule is live, it keeps running the old crawler image and spending money.
- The `south-st-pete-app-migrate` ECR repo and its task definition.
- #140's credential in IAM tag metadata is still open, unrelated to this change.

Dropped, not moved: the scraping-etiquette rules (respect robots.txt, rate-limit, honest User-Agent, treat a block as a stop signal) went away with `crawler.md`. If Phase D adds any outbound fetching of funder sites, restore them from git history as a binding rule first.

Why: Amber's direction. The grounded-RAG core (threshold, dedup, cite-or-refuse) is exactly what questionnaire 5.4 asks of the drafting engine, so keeping it beats starting over. Everything source-specific was dead weight for the new product.
Date: 2026-10-19

## #159 — Amber's sign-off on the four open design questions from #158's plan

Decision: Amber answered on 2026-10-19:
1. **No-invented-claims rule (questionnaire 5.4): approved as designed.** Every number, date, dollar amount and partner name in a draft must appear in a cited source chunk (a deterministic check, not model judgment). Anything unsupported is flagged, never filled in. Her reasoning: Erica reviews every application before submission and supplies correct information for whatever is flagged, so flags are the right hand-off to a human, and the human review step is load-bearing in this design.
2. **Purge the old civic-records corpus from RDS: approved.** It covers `chunks` rows from the St. Petersburg sources and test uploads, plus the orphaned `crawl_runs` and `granicus_transcription_jobs` tables. It runs as an ordered migration through the B0 migration runner, ahead of C2's `chunks.org_id NOT NULL`. It is not run ad hoc, because RDS is reachable only from inside AWS and the old one-off migrate task was removed in #158.
3. **Email notifications from a system address: approved.** The app sends assignment and deadline notifications from a dedicated system sender (provider TBD in its own entry; SES fits the AWS stack). Per questionnaire 6.6, notifications carry the assignment, the due date and a link, never sensitive attachments or credentials. This does not authorize sending from, or reading, FLFPC's own mailbox (questionnaire 3.3 stays out of scope).
4. **AWS cleanup: approved to inspect.** Read the live state, then prepare exact teardown commands for Amber to run. Each teardown still gets recorded when it's executed.

Why: these were the blocking open questions in the B–F design plan (§10 items 1, 3, 7, 8).
